# FireSim — Project Record

Living record of the work, decisions, references and testing behind **FireSim**, the
Canadian FBP wildfire spread simulator for municipal EOC planning. This file in the repo
(`docs/PROJECT_RECORD.md`) is the source of truth; copies attached elsewhere are snapshots.
Append to it; don't rewrite history. Compiled 2026-10-08 from the repository, its docs, the
git history and the owner's recorded decisions.

- **Live:** https://wildfire-simulator.vercel.app (frontend, Vercel) · API https://firesim-api.fly.dev/api/v1/health (Fly.io `firesim-api`, region yyz)
- **Repo:** https://github.com/Tphambolio/wildfire-simulator (local: `~/dev/wildfire/wildfire-simulator-v3`), branch `master`
- **Owner:** Travis Kennedy, P.Ag (City of Edmonton)
- **Tracked in Claude for Science project** `proj_4c36553a0c0c` (snapshots of this record are attached there; this file stays the source of truth)
- **Status at this revision:** branch `fix/reattach-after-reload` (from `master` `b50720d`, after PR #95): **reload re-attach, verified and edge cases closed** (backlog E1, owner-approved 2026-10-24). PR #91 [R56] already re-attaches the run on screen after a reload (sessionStorage `firesim-v3-live-run`, this tab only); verified on `master` with the mocked API for single runs with an ensemble, multi-day runs reloaded on day 2, perimeter restarts and reloads after completion. Fixed: a run the server no longer has after a reload showed "Run restored after reload" over a failed run plus an error toast that spoke of an operational period; it now says one plain sentence in the Situation panel and starts clean. New `tests/e2e/reattach.spec.ts` (6 tests, incl. the first test of the "Reload run" flag) and 2 Vitest cases. Open (owner): a closed tab or a new tab does not re-attach (sessionStorage is per tab, by #91's design).
- **Status at this revision:** branch `feat/harness-start-strata` (from `master` `4ecb2f9`, after PR #91): **start-type strata and start-perimeter diagnostics in the CFSDS validation harness** (backlog A2, owner-approved 2026-10-18; [R58]). Every fire-day record carries a start type (point / fresh perimeter / mature perimeter, from CFSDS DOB before the burn day only), the observed static share of the start perimeter ([R53] Fig. 4 method) and, per window, the modelled static share and the growth ratio (modelled ÷ observed growth beyond the start perimeter); `validate.py report` / `compare` add per-stratum tables. Descriptive: no score, decision or headline changes (the 143-fire-day baseline reproduces the [R45] C1 run exactly, 0 mismatches in 22,308 values). Tests: engine 1401 passed (21 new), API 304 passed.
- **Status at this revision:** branch `fix/alberta-permanent-utc6` (from `master` `4ecb2f9`, after PR #91): **Alberta's permanent UTC-6 clock from 2026-11-01** (backlog §F, owner-approved 2026-10-18; label "MT" chosen by the owner) ([R57]). Wall clock: MST/MDT before 2026-11-01 02:00 MDT, UTC-6 "MT" after, applied by the app and API themselves so stale browser/server tz data cannot fall back; fire-weather day (noon LST) kept at 19 UTC (CWFIS `tz_correct` -7), one setting `FIRESIM_AB_FWI_LST_OFFSET_H`; API image pins pip `tzdata>=2026.3`. Pyra switched in the companion PR on `Tphambolio/FWI` (same branch name); merge both together.
- **Previous status:** branch `fix/field-test-round4` (from `master` `272dd62`, after PRs #86, #88 and #89): **round-4 browser field test fixes** ([R56], live Chrome tester on the #86 build, 2026-10-18). ICS 209-WF no longer freezes the tab (previewed in the EOC console, nothing prints by itself, map snapshot wait bounded); a reload during a run re-attaches to it by its ID (no passphrase); the clocks carry the calendar day after midnight ("D2 03:32"); time left from the recent progress rate; About run time 1-15 min; grid runs show their burned fuel mix; the station notice shows old → new; Clear results clears the header chips; round-3 R1 (type-ahead neighbourhood picker) done. Tests: tsc 0 errors; Vitest 473 passed (44 files); Playwright 75/75 (5 new in `field-test-r4.spec.ts`, incl. a 145-frame 72 h run); engine and API untouched.
- **Previous status:** branch `docs/rescore-backcast-e1-ensemble` (from `master` `2686680`, after PRs #82, #84 and #85): **ensemble part (E1) of the pre-registered rescore on the pre-fire (back-cast) fuel grid completed and scored** ([R49]; `PREREG_RESCORE_BACKCAST.md` §4 E1, §7.8, `a5da766`). The record's 20-member ensemble (A15 sizes, seed 1) on the same 65 held-out fire-days, both grids rerun on identical code with identical member perturbations. CRPS of log10 area 0.465 → **0.407** (−0.057 [−0.113, −0.007], back-cast better); P10-P90 coverage 48 → 49 % (+1.5 pp [−7.1, +10.5], no detectable difference) → rule met, **ensemble statement updated for historical Alberta replays** (coverage 49 %, CRPS 0.407, CRPSS +0.24, spread/skill 1.34, P10 ≥ 90 % on 28 %); 2014b kept as comparator; still too narrow and over-confident, P10 not a worst case. Docs only (§3, §4.2, §4.4, §6, §7; `docs/validation.md`; `docs/OVERVIEW.md`); no code change.
- **Previous status:** branch `fix/field-test-round3` (from `master` `3bec116`, after PR #78; rebased on `master` `61dafdd`, after PRs #79, #80 and #84): **round-3 browser field test fixes** (Claude in Chrome tester, 2026-10-13; R2-R12). The IAP no longer writes objectives, command emphasis, strategy or resources (ENTER placeholders plus labelled model context); multi-day arrival times keep their true time and show the day ("D2 16:25"); Setup (ignition, start, weather and its source, curing, options) is kept per incident across a reload; an automatic station-weather fill says what it replaced, with Undo; a start outside the burning period is warned; multi-day runs report "Day 2 of 3" progress with a rough time left, a WebSocket heartbeat and robust polling; a run that ends short of its hours says so and offers Reload run; EOC/IAP weather and run span from the run inputs; evacuation-status export has local times.
- **Previous status:** PR #84, branch `feat/rescore-backcast` (from `master` `f4cdd17`, after PRs #79 and #80): **headline spread validation rescored on the pre-fire (back-cast) fuel grid** (owner-approved 2026-10-15, "rescore validation after 79"; [R45]). Pre-registered (`docs/PREREG_RESCORE_BACKCAST.md`, `a5da766`, tag `prereg-rescore-backcast-2026-10-15`, pushed before scoring; the primary overlaps #79 in full, disclosed). Same 143 fire-days, code and options; only the fuel differs (CFS 2014b vs the Alberta 2025 grid back-cast to each fire's start); both arms rerun. Held-out 17 h F1, chosen set-up: 0.212 → **0.246** (+0.034 [+0.007, +0.058], better) → **headline now 0.246 on the back-cast grid** (historical Alberta replays); whole perimeter 0.123 → 0.147, Bennett + spin-up 0.250 → 0.294, every configuration better; over-prediction (92 → 94 % of days, whole perimeter) and area bias unchanged; set-up gain +0.089 → +0.099 (difference +0.010 [−0.016, +0.030]); ensemble rescore (E1) not completed (16-18 of 65 days; no partial score); no fire-day lacks an epoch. New harness option `scripts/validate.py --domains-dir` (+ `wise_fireday.py --out/--tag`). Suppression strata exploratory (§4.4).
- **Previous status:** PR #79, branch `feat/prefire-fuel-grid` (from `master` `3bec116`, after PR #78; rebased on `master` `fff5104`, after PR #80): **pre-fire (back-cast) fuel for historical replays** (owner-approved 2026-10-12, [R39-fuel] design, [R43] bias check, [R44] build and tests). A run dated in or before 2025 whose ignition gets an Alberta-grid window has the cells NBAC records as burned on or after its date (the replayed fire's own scar and every later scar; restore key = latest recorded end date, else the end of the burn year) restored to fuel typed from the SCANFI v2 epoch strictly before the scenario's year with the CFS national FBP decision rules (Swystun, Taylor & Simpson 2018); prebuilt per epoch (`scripts/fuelgrid/backcast.py`; burn key + epochs 2010/2015/2020, 18.7 MB release `data-ab-prefire-v1` fetched by `api/Dockerfile`, image not volume), read by window (`firesim.data.backcast`, `FuelSources.backcast`); runs report `fuel_source.backcast` and the fuel-vintage warning (PR #78) shows the applied note or the reason. **Pre-registered re-scoring** (`PREREG` `3582102`, tag `prereg-backcast-2026-10-12`) of the [R27] fire-days, held-out 17 h F1: back-cast 0.246 vs scar-masked 2025 grid 0.160, **+0.086 [+0.046, +0.128], better** (primary); vs CFS 2014b +0.034 [+0.007, +0.058], better; 2014b fill instead of SCANFI −0.026 [−0.053, +0.001], no detectable difference. Slave Lake SWF-065 (owner inputs, 47 km/h): SWF-065 polygon 80 % O-1 → 54 % M / 26 % D / 6 % C / 3 % O-1; area by 23:50 520 → 183 ha; town CSD 500 m 20:55 → 20:20 (head follows a 100 m O-1 band beside Highway 2 outside the NBAC burn; documented: town ~17:25 May 15).
- **Previous status:** PR #80, branch `fix/long-run-ids` (from `master` `3bec116`, after PR #78): **long run IDs** (owner, Travis Kennedy, EOC Chief of Plans, approved 2026-10-13). New run IDs are 32 lowercase hex characters, 128 random bits from `secrets.token_hex(16)`, made only by `api/src/firesim_api/services/run_ids.py` (single, multi-day, perimeter-override; ensembles share the run's ID); IDs made before 2026-10-13 (8 hex, 32 bits) stay valid for GET, `/arrival`, `/ensemble`, WebSocket, labels, delete, the archive and incidents saved in browsers or exported. Unknown IDs back off per IP (`middleware/run_probe_limit.py`: 20 free "Simulation not found" answers, then 2, 4, 8 ... s up to 300 s, 429 / WebSocket 4029; found runs never count). Tests in §5.
- **Previous status:** branch `feat/restart-weather-schedule` (from `master` `1481702`): **RPAS perimeter restart fixes, weather that changes during a run, fuel-grid vintage warning** (owner-approved items A and B and issue 6 of the owner's SWF-065 field tests, [R41] [R42]). (1) Restarts use Setup's current weather, FWI codes and weather changes (the source run's only when none is sent; `weather_used`); (2) Predict N h runs N h: the "cap" and the last missing frame were WebSocket frames lost when the socket closed before they were sent (reproduced on the live site; the handler is now the socket's only writer, `n_frames` on completion); (3) community and asset arrivals carry across restarts; (4) restarts load buildings like normal runs (mask, exposure, house-to-house; missing before); (5) restart growth: the 1,307 vs 900 ha gap was mainly the missing building mask (Slave Lake grid, 47 km/h: restart from its own 2 h perimeter 997.5 → 598.8 ha vs continuous 496.5); the rest is the restart front rebuilt at cell edges (+1-2 % uniform fuel, +7 % mixed); (6) the loaded perimeter clears with Clear results and names its source run; no duplicate start frame; header chips labelled as run values; (B) manual weather schedule (step changes) in Setup, API `weather_schedule`, run as an hourly stream (FFMC/ISI recomputed hourly), in the ensemble, restarts, archive, exports and ICS 209; fuel-grid vintage warning in Setup, run summary, exports and 209 remarks. Demo, not validation: the SWF-065 two-stage case in one run (§4.4). Tests §5.
- **Previous revision:** PR #77, branch `feat/saved-runs` (from `master` `b16bbe4`, after PR #76): **saved runs, run archive phase 2** (owner-approved 2026-10-12: the run list is shared behind one passphrase). New `GET /api/v1/simulations` (archived + in-memory runs, newest first, `limit`/`offset`, filters by time, box around the ignition and kind), `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/v1/simulations/{id}` (label ≤ 80 characters / remove a finished run); all three need `X-FireSim-Passphrase` = `FIRESIM_RUNS_PASSPHRASE` (SHA-256 + `hmac.compare_digest`; unset → 403, nothing listed; per-IP backoff → 429). Optional run `label` on the run requests; later labels in the new index table `run_labels` (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` migration). App: Setup → Saved runs (passphrase in sessionStorage only, cleared on 401/403; Edmonton times, label, ignition point to 3 decimals, kind, area, duration, fuel source; "near current map view" and date filters; click reloads the run through `restoreRun`; rename; delete with confirmation) and a "Run label (optional)" field. `GET /simulations/{id}` stays open. Not deployed until merged; the owner sets the secret (`docs/run-archive.md` "Phase 2").
- **Previous status:** branch `feat/building-size-licence` (from `master` `1481702`, after PR #75): **size rule for building counts and a licence review** (owner delegated both decisions 2026-10-12, [R40]). (1) Footprints **under 20 m² are outbuildings**: they stay in the structure-spread graph and the building mask and carry fire, but are counted separately from every headline count (structure spread involved/front/b2b/ember/burning/burnt out, exposure bands, inside perimeter) as `units_small_involved` / `small_within_500m`; applies to every source; `FIRESIM_SMALL_FOOTPRINT_M2=0` restores the former counts exactly (checked on 6 sites). Edge runs, ODB vs Microsoft involvement: Lethbridge 943 vs 470 → 567 vs 464, Grande Prairie 365 vs 151 → 251 vs 151, Canmore 288 vs 200 → 230 vs 199, Cochrane 312 vs 122 → 308 vs 122; Edmonton: Terwillegar identical, live request 43 → 42 involved. (2) Licences: Lethbridge v2.0 **OK with a notice** (indemnity binds each user for their own use; notice in `docs/data-sources.md` and the release-notes draft); Grande Prairie **OK as is** (v1.0 text on a City ArcGIS item modified 2026-10-05; Policy 702 still published); Edmonton 2025 LiDAR: **no classified point cloud is on data.edmonton.ca** (the 2025 items are 0.2 m GeoTIFF rasters), so the grid built from a City-supplied classified copy is not clearly covered by the licence text — **kept with a corrected notice; written City confirmation recommended** (draft in [R40] §6.2, not sent).
- **Previous status:** branch `docs/edmonton-grid-licence` (from `master` `b9df2c9`, after PR #73): **the City of Edmonton LiDAR fuel grid is open data and stays** (owner decision 2026-10-12, [R38]). Licence check: its inputs are all City of Edmonton open data under the Open Government Licence – Edmonton — the 2025 LiDAR (data.edmonton.ca `sz3i-y5ze`, flown 29-30 June 2025), uPLVI 2025, Naturalized Areas, and the Trees inventory that labelled the conifer/deciduous crown classifier; no 2019 LiDAR or internal tree table in the chain. About credits the grid ("Contains information licensed under the Open Government Licence – Edmonton"); §4.3 row, [R21] correction, §6 licence item closed with residual checks. Comparison with the open-data grid v2.1 (10 Edmonton ignitions, 4 h, structure spread, 15 May and 15 July): grids agree on 74 % of mapped cells (open grid: M-2 243 vs 1,746 ha, D-2 3,052 vs 2,129, O-1a 20,348 vs 16,984, O-1b 3,583 vs 7,644); run totals 978 → 1,033 ha and 160 → 165 ha, units involved 681 → 552 and 322 → 283, large per-run differences in both directions. v2.1 provenance: its Edmonton / St. Albert stand objects used a conifer model trained on City LiDAR crown labels (no longer a licence issue). No data added, removed or replaced; frontend text only.
- **Previous status:** branch `feat/odb-municipal-buildings` (from `master` `a1ff495`, merged with `master` after PRs #69/#70): **municipal building footprints and the Edmonton box decision** (owner-approved 2026-10-12, [R34]): StatCan ODB v3 municipal footprints replace Microsoft 2019 inside 9 census subdivisions (Calgary, Lethbridge, Parkland County outside the Edmonton box, Grande Prairie, Airdrie, Okotoks, Cochrane, Canmore, Banff; OGL-type licences), Microsoft elsewhere; Edmonton identical (19 selections, 6 footprint boxes, 10 full runs). Edmonton box dropped as a limit on buildings (structure spread reads every reachable building; City neighbourhoods keep buildings south of 53.39 N); `FIRESIM_EDMONTON_BOX_LEGACY=1` restores it. Release `data-ab-buildings-2026-10` is an owner step; until then the image falls back to the Microsoft file.
- **Previous status:** branch `docs/ab-fuel-grid-decisions` (from `master` `a1ff495`): **three Alberta fuel grid decisions** (owner asked 2026-10-12 to research, decide and document; [R35]): (1) Alberta code 31 O-1 stays **O-1b all year** (ST-X-3 p.16 gives matted grass as a spring condition, not a date rule; O-1b ≥ O-1a from ISI 8, ×1.21 at ISI 20; a date swap would also hit the City grid's own O-1a/O-1b labels); (2) code 105 "Vegetated Non-Fuel" stays **non-fuel**: measured as cropland (ABMI 2010: 46 % agriculture, 64 % in a community WUI proxy) and recent burns (63 % of 2024 burns, ~70 % of 2025 burns); pre-registered 2025 burn test (`PREREG_105`, `a61ac7e`) **void** because the published grid already re-types 2025 burns; (3) **recommend asking** Alberta Wildfire GIS for written OGL-Alberta confirmation for the image service and the build rules (draft in [R35]; not sent). No behaviour change; comments, docs, 3 new tests.
- **Previous status (PR #69):** branch `feat/hamlets-localities` (PR #69, from `master` `a1ff495`): **hamlets and localities as community units outside Edmonton** (owner-approved task 2026-10-12, [R36]): new bundled layer `frontend/public/alberta/places.geojson` (1,388 units, 711 KB / 113 KB gzipped; `scripts/build_ab_places.py`): 479 hamlets (Alberta hamlet boundaries 434 + urban service areas 4, OGL – Alberta; CGNDB hamlet points 41, OGL – Canada) and 909 localities (StatCan 2021 designated places not covered by a hamlet 87, StatCan Open Licence; CGNDB localities 822 as 250 m discs). Units are named "Calahoo (hamlet, Sturgeon County)"; a county arrival defers to a hamlet or locality at the crossing, otherwise the county dot (PR #67) stays. Edmonton unchanged. Frontend only; API unchanged.
- **Previous status (PR #65):** branch `feat/run-archive` (from `master` `27f0261`, PR #62): **run archive, phase 1** (owner-approved option A, [R28] §6): finished runs are archived (SQLite index + local or R2 object store) and GET `/simulations/{id}`, `/arrival`, `/ensemble` fall back to the archive after a deploy or restart (`archived: true`, otherwise the live body); retention 365 days; Litestream backup of the index to R2 when configured [R30]. Not deployed durably yet: volume, bucket and secrets need the owner (commands in `docs/run-archive.md`).
- **Previous status (PR #64):** branch `feat/alberta-assets-roads-terrain` (from `master` `30d7051`): **critical assets, major roads and terrain for all of Alberta** (owner-approved task 2026-10-11): Alberta critical assets (3,901; StatCan ODHF 2020 + OpenStreetMap; same categories and ICS keys as Edmonton plus `ems`) and major roads (17,184 OSM pieces) as FlatGeobuf files served by area (`GET /api/v1/layers/assets|roads`; the app asks for ~28 km around an ignition and keeps the bundled Edmonton layers inside their box); NRCan MRDEM-30 elevation averaged to ~100 m as a 91.7 MB COG, slope/aspect derived per run window (Edmonton DEM unchanged on the City lattice; runs report `terrain_source`); release `data-ab-infra-2026-10` (owner step) fetched by the Dockerfile with SHA-256; local API peak RSS 657 → 686 MB on 7 runs, current RSS after outside-Edmonton runs −50 MB (Edmonton DEM no longer loaded there); Hinton +5 % area with slope, Canmore 180 → 26 ha (run turned upslope into non-fuel) [R29]; tests §5.
- **Previous status:** branch `analysis/opendata-grid-fire-test` (from `master` `3abdf3b`, merged with `master` `b85686d`): **pre-registered northern Alberta fire test of the open-data fuel grid** (owner-approved 2026-10-10; `PREREG` committed `e5af3a7` before scoring): on the 16 held-out CFSDS fires the open-data v1 grid (current inputs, burn scars masked) scores 17 h F1 0.192 vs 0.212 for the national 2014b grid (ΔF1 −0.020, 95 % CI −0.066 to +0.023: no detectable difference) and beats CFS 2026 masked (0.170; +0.021, +0.002 to +0.044); pre-fire build on 2021-2024 fires 0.236 vs 0.202 (+0.034, −0.008 to +0.082); the grid maps most boreal conifer as O-1b [R27]. New scripts only (`scripts/fuelgrid/fire_areas.py`, `scripts/validation/opendata_fire_test.py`); no engine change.
- **Previous status (PR #54):** branch `feat/fuelgrid-full-fbp-key` (PR #54, merged with `master` `1b377b9`, PR #55): **open-data fuel grid v2.1 merged as data/scripts only** — `scripts/fuelgrid/` v2 key (`key2.py`, `sources2.py`, `build2.py`, `validate2.py`) and v2.1 stand objects with the uPLVI-trained stand model (`stands.py`), tests in `engine/tests/fuelgrid/`, docs in `docs/fuel-grid-open-data.md` [R26]. **No app default changes:** the API and app keep the City LiDAR grid in Edmonton and the Alberta Wildfire grid elsewhere (PR #55); nothing under `api/`, `engine/src/` or `frontend/` is touched by this branch. Adoption of v2.1 waits on a check of the Edmonton D-2 share (80 % of forest) against fire records and on the northern fire test (§3, §8).
- **Previous status (PR #55):** branch `feat/ab-wildfire-fuel-default` (from `master` `8743ad5`, merged with `master` `399cb46`, PRs #56 and #57): **Alberta Wildfire FBP fuel types 2025 (100 m) are the default fuel outside the City grid** (owner decision 2026-10-10, §3): City LiDAR grid wherever it has data (its 50 m lattice and the 20 m window unchanged), Alberta grid in its no-data cells and in a 40 km window around ignitions elsewhere in Alberta, uniform fuel outside Alberta (no national grid bundled); D-1/D-2 and M-1/M-2 by date (day 150-257 leaf-on), per-cell percent conifer, O-1 → O-1b [H], 105 vegetated non-fuel → non-fuel; runs report `fuel_source`; COG 31.8 MB shipped as a release asset fetched by the Dockerfile (not deployed); peak RSS 290 → 417 MB on the test requests; burn-probability route fixed (wrong function since 2026-10-05); indicative check on 7 calibration fire-days F1 0.287 → 0.295, confounded by post-fire re-typing [R24]; tests: engine 1187 passed / 1 xfailed, API 207 passed / 1 skipped, Vitest 333 (§5).
- **Previous status (PR #57):** branch `feat/structure-veg-roof` (from `master` `8743ad5`, merged with `master` `0d1f728`, PR #56): structure step 3, all opt-in (spec §2.1, §4.6, §6.2, C22-C24): per-building open-data vegetation attributes (Meta 1 m CHM; context only), the vegetation-bridged cutoff (`structure_vegetation_bridge`: links ≤ 20 m, 20-45 m only across ≥ 20 % gap woody cover) and the combustible-roof scenario (`structure_combustible_roof_share` 0/5/15/30 %, ember threshold × 0.375, "scenario … (not observed roofs)"); distance + vegetation baseline; pre-registered tests (`PREREG_STEP3` `492c7af`, Eaton `cde682e`, Camp `cf33136`, all pushed before scoring). Results: the bridge did not beat plain Hamada or the baselines on Eaton 2025 (independent), Camp 2018 or Jasper; the roof scenario changed nothing anywhere (no ember ignition) [R23].
- **Previous status (PR #56):** branch `fix/rerun-and-multiday` (from `master` `8743ad5`): owner bugs of 2026-10-11. (1) Re-running: every new run (single, multi-day, perimeter restart) and Clear results stop the old run's ensemble poll and ask the server to stop computing it (new `DELETE /simulations/{id}/ensemble`); a single run with the hourly forecast clears the old run when Run is pressed; the reported stale view did not reproduce against the mocked API on `8743ad5` (§3). (2) Multi-day speed: Huygens front ~2x faster with bit-identical frames (O(n²) resampling, redundant sort, per-vertex wavelets), DEM held as compact rows (Edmonton DEM ~560 → ~76 MB; 3-day grid run peak RSS 713 → 425 MB), multi-day `cells_mode` incremental (3-day grid run 21.5 → 1.65 MB streamed); an abandoned ensemble on the 1-vCPU server was the likely cause of the live slowness. Also fixes a GET/WebSocket race that could report "completed" with the last frames missing (found by CI). Tests: engine 1124 passed / 1 xfailed, API 201 passed / 1 skipped, Vitest 340, Playwright `rerun.spec.ts` 2 + `ensemble`/`smoke` 4 (§5). Previous revision (PR #53): branch `feat/structure-burnout` (from `master` `80ccbe5`, merged with `master` `ac29edc`): building burn-out (spec §4.3, C21; API `structure_burnout`, default on): a unit stops passing fire (Hamada crossings and embers) when its design fire ends, 66 min (150 kW/m²) or 70 min (400) after involvement; counts split burning / burnt out, detail `t_out_h`, app card rows, chart and map styling. Jasper pre-registered (`PREREG_BURNOUT`, `5753b8a`, pushed before scoring): primary identical to Hamada alone (κ 0.472 vs distance band 0.516), 11 of 12 sensitivity runs identical, cutoff 45 m 0.508 → 0.500; decision rule not met. Edmonton: 0 % to −10 % involved at 6 h; live request 93 → 68 involved, peak RSS 1,088 MB [R19]; tests: after merging `master` `ac29edc` (PR #52) engine 1114 passed / 1 xfailed, API 196 passed / 1 skipped, Vitest 333 (§5). Previous revision (PR #52): **M5 implemented** (branch `feat/wui-20m-window`, merged with `master` `fcfa8db`): fuel rasters coarsened by majority class (was nearest neighbour); grid runs near buildings repeated on the native 20 m cells in a window around the 50 m fire, within memory guards, else the 50 m run (frames carry `grid`). Uniform fuel: 20 m converges on the FBP ellipse (area error −4 to −8 % vs −4 to −10 % at 50 m). Structure front-contact gap median 22-42 m → 9-15 m (max 64-78 → 24-30 m); Edmonton areas +0 to +410 % at 20 m (fuel detail, not the building mask); peak RSS +≤ 5 MB (1.08-1.09 GB) [R18]; tests 2026-10-10: engine 1100 passed / 1 xfailed, API 156 passed, Vitest 327, Playwright 34; after merging `master` `fcfa8db` (PR #50): engine 1100 passed / 1 xfailed, API 192 passed / 1 skipped, Vitest 329 (§5). Previous revision (PR #50): branch `feat/pyra-starting-codes` merged with `master` `3abdf3b` (PRs #48, #49, #46 and #51 merged 2026-10-10): starting FWI codes from Pyra's chain. `/api/v1/weather/current` has a first tier "pyra" (Alberta) that reproduces what Pyra's station page shows today for the nearest Pyra station (Pyra's published CWFIS carry-over stepped one day with the GEM noon-LST weather), so the fire-weather product and the prediction start from the same numbers; CWFIS → archive → GEM stays as the fallback (§3, §4.1, §4.3). Parity with Pyra's own engine on 15 recorded cases and live against Pyra's station page for four Alberta stations: identical to ≤ 1e-14 (§5). Tests 2026-10-10 after merging `master` `3abdf3b`: engine 1076 passed / 1 xfailed, API 188 passed / 1 skipped (the Pyra file 37/37 with `PYRA_DIR`), Vitest 326; Playwright left to CI (§5). Previous revision (PR #46): branch `feat/structure-embers` merged with `master` `c662836` (PRs #44, #45, #47, #49 and #48 merged 2026-10-10): opt-in ember ignition of buildings (spec §6.1; API `structure_embers`, app "Ember ignition"); published check values reproduced; Jasper ember check pre-registered (`ccce4d6`): no building ignited by embers in 17 runs, κ unchanged at 0.472 vs 0.516 distance band; Edmonton rerun: 0 ember ignitions in 42 runs; at the 150 kW/m² default the ember stage is effectively inert (owner decision open) [R16]; tests 2026-10-10: engine 967 passed / 1 xfailed, API 117, Vitest 285, Playwright 30 (§5). Previous: PR #43 memory fix and house-to-house UI [R11]; PR #42 Jasper check [R10]. Previous revision (PR #48): branch `feat/ui-declutter` merged with `master` `35e8c78` (PRs #44, #45, #47 and #49 merged 2026-10-10): decluttered UI (accessible tooltips, short badges, About & sources tab), main-run progress bar (WebSocket `simulation.status`), re-run at a new ignition without a reload, weather kept when the ignition moves; tests 2026-10-10: engine 943 passed / 1 xfailed, API 119, Vitest 316, Playwright 34 (§5). Previous revision (PR #47): branch `fix/fire-weather-path` merged with `master` `e779fa1` (PRs #44 and #45 merged 2026-10-10): fire weather path fixes from the FireSim vs Pyra comparison [R15] (DC cold-day rule [R15] M1 + daily FWI oracle test; current-weather path [R15] M6-M8; GEM pinned [R15] M3; coefficient 147.2 in the frontend too); tests 2026-10-10: engine 997 passed / 1 xfailed, API 125, Vitest 284, Playwright 30 (§5). Previous revision (PR #44): branch `feat/curing-default-and-citations` merged with `master` `4e5d20b` (PRs #42, #43 and #45 merged 2026-10-10). Mechanics decisions M1-M6 recorded (§3, [R12], [R13]). M1 implemented: grass curing defaults to 95 % from day of year 60 to 149 (snow-melt to green-up) and has no default outside it (API 422 when grass can burn; UI field required). Validated on CFSDS: held-out F1 unchanged (0.2126 → 0.2120) [R14]. M3 rewords the area-bias open item. Citation fixes (flame length, FTR-33, Fox-Hughes, Alexander 2010, Tran 1992, Beverly 2010, ICS forms, ellipse). Tests 2026-10-10 (§5). Previous revision (PR #45): branch `fix/ensemble-empty-member` (from `master` `cbbffe2`): fixes the 2026-10-10 production ensemble failure (`IndexError` in `_window` when a member's ignition cell could not spread); ensembles now survive failing members (statistics over finished members, zero-burn members included; failed when < 50 % finish); tests: engine 950 passed / 1 xfailed, API 115, Vitest 282, Playwright 30 (§5). Before that: branch `fix/structure-memory-ui` merged with `master` `67ac021` (PR #42, Jasper check, merged): structure spread builds units only where it can reach (fixes the 2026-10-10 out-of-memory crash of the API: peak 1.76 GB → 1.09 GB, identical counts) with a memory guard; house-to-house spread in the app (opt-in option, counts, chart, per-building map layer; owner reversed D3 on 2026-10-10) [R11]; tests 2026-10-10: engine 940 passed / 1 xfailed, API 112, Vitest 282, Playwright 30 (§5). Jasper 2024 check (PR #42): κ 0.47 vs 0.52 for a distance baseline, pre-registered rule not met ([R10], §4.4).

---

## 1. Purpose and audience

FireSim simulates wildfire growth and fire behaviour with the Canadian Forest Fire Behavior
Prediction (FBP) System and reports where and when the modelled fire reaches neighbourhoods,
critical assets, roads and buildings.

- **Primary user:** the EOC **Planning section** (Situation Unit, Plans Chief), plus emergency
  management and fire officers trained in the FBP System. The main screen is built around
  **resource deployment**: head / flank / back, head fire intensity class and what it means for
  suppression, clock-time arrivals.
- **Intended use now:** preparedness, training, tabletop exercises and what-if planning.
- **Later goal:** operational fire-growth prediction during incidents with Prometheus/WISE-level
  accuracy in real time, once validation on observed fires supports it. Not there yet (§4.4).
- **What it is not:** an evacuation decision tool (it never suggests Order / Alert / Watch; the
  Planning section sets them), a structure-loss or ignition-probability model, a source of
  firefighter, aircraft or RPAS safety distances, or a replacement for the lead agency's
  prediction.
- **Disclaimers the product must carry:** "planning tool" limits badge; outputs shared beyond
  Planning state issuer, run time, model version (`/api/v1/version` git SHA) and inputs; a single
  run is not the expected fire (`docs/model-card.md`, "Responsible use").

## 2. Architecture

Frontend → HTTP/WebSocket → API → engine; frames are streamed back over WebSocket
(`docs/architecture.md`). The engine is pure Python with no web dependencies.

| File / module | Role |
|---|---|
| `engine/src/firesim/fbp/` (`calculator.py`, `constants.py`, `crown_fire.py`) | FBP System, 18 fuel types (ST-X-3 + GLC-X-10), verified against `cffdrs`; vectorised path `fbp_ellipse_arrays` |
| `engine/src/firesim/fwi/` (`calculator.py`, `classes.py`) | FWI System (Van Wagner & Pickett 1985), hourly FFMC; CWFIS FWI map classes |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/cellular.py` | Level-set grid model (ELMFIRE-style Huygens velocity from each cell's FBP ellipse); per-cell arrival, speed, head/flank/back; active edges; flame panels |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/huygens.py`, `ellipse.py`, `slope.py` | Huygens wavelets on uniform fuel; shared FBP layer `fbp_for_conditions`; ellipse geometry; slope factor |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/diurnal.py` | Opt-in burning period and hourly-FFMC spin-up from 17:00 local |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/ensemble.py`, `montecarlo.py` | Ensemble P10/P50/P90 arrival + burn probability; simple Monte Carlo burn probability |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/albini.py`, `spotting.py` | Albini/Chase/Morris maximum spotting distance; heuristic emission/landing |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/simulator.py` | `Simulator` orchestrator; picks grid model whenever a fuel grid exists |
| `engine/src/firesim/exposure.py` | Building exposure: distance bands, Cohen (2004) radiant flux, flux-time index |
| `engine/src/firesim/structures/` | Structure-to-structure spread (opt-in, illustrative; `embers.py` since 2026-10-10: opt-in ember ignition, design fire, Himoto/Sardoy transport, ψ criterion, spec §6.1): `units.py` builds one unit per building footprint (centroid, footprint in local metres, area, square-equivalent size, neighbour graph with edge-to-edge separations within a cutoff, default 30 m); `hamada.py` Hamada rates; `spread.py` front contact (from the building's grid cells) + building-to-building Dijkstra (API `structure_spread`, default off), units built only for the area the spread can reach (`structure_spread_for_grid_run`, footprint source = `BuildingIndex` or a list; memory guard 60,000 units), involved-unit detail for the map; specification `docs/structure-spread-spec.md` |
| `engine/src/firesim/data/` | Fuel loader (code schemes incl. Edmonton canopy LiDAR, drone pipeline, CFS national), DEM loader, reprojection (`raster_grid.py`), WUI/water/building masks, synthetic demo grid |
| `engine/src/firesim/validation/` | CFSDS validation harness (`cfsds.py`, `harness.py`, `metrics.py`, `report.py`, `weather.py`) |
| `api/src/firesim_api/` | FastAPI: `routers/` (simulations, fwi, weather, health), `services/runner.py` (background runs, grid cache), `schemas/`, `ws/` |
| `frontend/src/` | React 19 + TypeScript + Vite + MapLibre GL: `MapView`, Setup sections, Situation panel (`FireMetrics` run details, `EvacStatusPanel`, `CriticalAssetsPanel`, `EOCSummary` actions), `TimeSlider`, `EOCConsole` (ICS forms, ICS Canada 209-WF, full EOC summary), `AboutPanel` (About & sources tab); shared tables `utils/fireClasses.ts`, `utils/fwiClass.ts`, `utils/time.ts` |
| `frontend/src/components/StructureSpreadPanel.tsx`, `InfoTip.tsx`, `utils/structureSpread.ts` | House-to-house spread in the app: Situation card (counts, stacked chart with the timeline cursor, map toggle), map layer `struct-units-*` in `MapView.tsx` (involved footprints by mechanism, legend, hover/click popup), caveat in a keyboard-accessible tooltip |
| `frontend/src/components/InfoTip.tsx`, `Badge.tsx`, `FwiClassChip.tsx`, `HfiClassChip.tsx`, `content/explanations.ts`, `styles/infotip.css` | Accessible explanation pop-ups (toggletip; `TipCore`, `InfoTip`, `TipButton`, `Badge`) and the single source of all tip/badge text (shared with the About tab and tests) |
| `frontend/src/components/AboutPanel.tsx`, `styles/about.css` | "About & sources" tab: intended use, limits and validation, methods and references, data and licences, responsible use, ICS Canada forms, help, version |
| `frontend/src/components/RunProgress.tsx`, `hooks/useSimulation.ts`; `api/.../services/runner.py` (`SimulationRun.set_phase`, `RunCancelled`), `routers/simulations.py` (`simulation.status`); engine `Simulator(progress=)`, `run_cellular_simulation(progress=)` | Main-run progress (phases, spread fraction, cancel mid-computation); run tokens so a new run ignores the old socket and polls; `clearResults` |
| `frontend/src/utils/scenarioDefaults.ts` | Scenario fallback config, matching the Setup defaults (WUI modifiers and OSM water off) |
| `scripts/build_edmonton_assets.py` | Builds `frontend/public/edmonton/assets.geojson` (critical assets) from open sources |
| `scripts/build_edmonton_roads.py` | Builds `frontend/public/edmonton/roads.geojson` (motorway to secondary + ramps) from Overpass |
| `scripts/structure_sensitivity.py` | Structure-spread sensitivity (cutoff, contact distance, footprint size, front-contact rule) on Edmonton footprints; aggregate outputs written outside the repo [R7], [R8] |
| `scripts/jasper_structure_validation.py` | (`--embers`: the pre-registered ember-ignition check, `PREREG_EMBERS` [R16]) Pre-registered end-state check of the Hamada option on the Jasper 2024 townsite losses (structure-only, ember entry imposed). Downloads the Municipality of Jasper damage layer with `OBJECTID` and `Status` only; the cache and outputs live outside the repo (`~/dev/wildfire/validation-data/jasper2024/`) and are never committed [R10] |
| `scripts/build_building_vegetation.py` | Structure step 3 data (offline, open data only): `canopy-raster` (open-ground canopy share on ≈ 5 m cells from the Meta 1 m CHM → `data/edmonton_canopy_5m.tif`), `ring-metrics` (per-footprint canopy rings, overhang, stand distance for any area), `edmonton-table` (→ `data/edmonton_building_vegetation.csv.gz`) [R21], [R23] |
| `scripts/structure_step3_validation.py` | Pre-registered step-3 tests (`PREREG_STEP3`, `PREREG_STEP3_EATON`, `PREREG_STEP3_CAMP`): vegetation-bridged cutoff and roof scenario vs Hamada, distance band and distance + vegetation baselines; `independent --fire eaton|camp` (CAL FIRE DINS), `jasper` (non-independent check), `--dry-run` (model only, random outcomes) [R23] |
| `scripts/fuelgrid/` | Open-data FBP fuel-grid pipeline (fetch → Edmonton LiDAR labels → build → validate → engine check; `python -m scripts.fuelgrid.run all`): 20 m EPSG:3776 leaf-on / leaf-off grids, percent conifer and a per-cell rule band for Edmonton and St. Albert; downloads and outputs in `$FUELGRID_DATA` (outside the repo) [R17], `docs/fuel-grid-open-data.md`. **v2** (`key2.py`, `sources2.py`, `build2.py`, `validate2.py`; `run all2` / `firesim2`): full FBP key + disturbance, stand-level typing, northern Alberta test areas (EPSG:3400) [R26] |
| `scripts/validate.py`, `scripts/validation/` | Validation runs (prepare / run / report / compare), CFSDS fetch, optional WISE fire-day runs |

**External data the code calls at run time:** Open-Meteo forecast API (`frontend/src/services/api.ts`,
`api/.../routers/weather.py`); CWFIS GeoServer WFS (`routers/weather.py`, current station
weather/FWI); OSM raster tiles and OpenTopoMap tiles, optional Mapbox satellite (`MapView.tsx`);
Nominatim geocoding and Overpass (`WeatherPanel.tsx`, `services/overpass.ts`). Build/validation
time only: City of Edmonton Open Data (Socrata, incl. Parcel Addresses `ut27-nrpn`), Government
of Alberta continuing care list (open.alberta.ca), StatCan ODHF, Overpass
(`build_edmonton_assets.py`, `build_edmonton_roads.py`); Open-Meteo ERA5 archive (`validation/weather.py`); CFSDS (OSF),
CFS national FBP grids, MRDEM (`scripts/validation/`).

**Run storage (2026-10-11, [R30]; deployed 2026-10-12):** runs live in API memory (≤ `FIRESIM_MAX_STORED_RUNS`, 40) and, once finished, in the run archive (`api/.../services/archive.py`, `docs/run-archive.md`): a SQLite index (`FIRESIM_ARCHIVE_DB`, WAL; one row per run: status, times, request, git SHA, fuel/buildings source, area, duration, frames) and payloads in an object store (`<id>/response.json.gz` = the live GET body, `arrival.json.gz`, `ensemble.json.gz`, `meta.json`). **Deployed:** the local backend on a 1 GB Fly volume (`firesim_archive`, mounted at `/data`, archive at `/data/firesim-archive`; Fly daily snapshots kept 5 days), capped at 800 MB (`FIRESIM_ARCHIVE_MAX_BYTES`); `/api/v1/health` reports `durable: true, backend: local`. The live API runs one ensemble at a time (`FIRESIM_MAX_CONCURRENT_ENSEMBLES` 1; later ones wait as pending; PR #68). The S3-compatible offsite copy and the Litestream index backup exist in the code but are **not deployed** (owner decision 2026-10-12); a lost volume loses the archive. Reads: memory, then archive. Retention: 365 days and the byte cap, at startup and every 24 h. Without a volume: a temporary directory with a startup warning (`archive.durable: false`). Incidents, periods and annotations stay in browser `localStorage`. **Saved-runs list (phase 2, 2026-10-12):** `GET /api/v1/simulations` lists the index (plus in-memory runs not archived yet) behind the shared passphrase `FIRESIM_RUNS_PASSPHRASE`; labels set later sit in the index table `run_labels`; `DELETE` removes a run's row, label and payloads (`routers/runs.py`). Stored: model output and requests only; per-building fields are those the live GET already returns (map display only, never exported).

**Build / deploy:** push to `master` → GitHub Actions (`engine-tests (3.11)`, `engine-tests (3.12)`,
`frontend` required; `frontend-e2e` not required) → `deploy` job runs
`flyctl deploy --remote-only --build-arg GIT_SHA=…` (API image bundles `data/` at `/app/data`);
Vercel rebuilds the frontend from `master` (`docs/deployment.md`).

## 3. Decisions log

Owner decisions are dated from the owner's recorded notes and the commits that implemented
them. Future sessions must respect them; ask the owner before reversing any.

| Date | Decision | Why / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-05 | Label FireSim a planning and training tool, not an operational forecast | Spread not then validated against observed fires (`876cf94`, PR #1) |
| 2026-10-05 | FBP layer must match `cffdrs` to float precision; stochastic CA replaced by a deterministic level set | NRES 799 audit found many departures from ST-X-3 (`289a1d0`, `5061025`; `docs/verification.md` §4) |
| 2026-10-06 | WUI zone multipliers off by default unless sourced | 425 park buffers (spread ×0.7, intensity ×1.2, embers ×3.0) have no source or generating script (`aae819b`, PR #7) |
| 2026-10-06 | Head fire intensity classes 1-6 from Cole & Alexander (1995) with CWFIS HFI map limits; one shared table | Replaced three conflicting, partly unsourced schemes incl. an I-V scheme with US resource typing (`10ce32b`, PR #12) |
| 2026-10-06 | FWI classes = CWFIS national FWI map intervals (0-5 / 6-15 / 16-22 / 23-29 / 30+), one table for engine, API and UI; not an official danger rating | `43f0800` (PR #9); `engine/src/firesim/fwi/classes.py` |
| 2026-10-06 | RPAS stand-off distance and 2 km crown-fire personnel rule removed; generic "fire authority / SFOC / CARs" reminder only, no distances | Unsourced, could be read as permission (`5f3be56`, `5771c73`, PR #14; at the owner's direction) |
| 2026-10-06 | Never put SFOC numbers or SFOC condition IDs in the public repo | Owner rule (`CLAUDE.md`, City of Edmonton notes) |
| 2026-10-06 | EOC redesign: primary user is the EOC Planning section; main screen built around resource deployment; desktop/laptop only; dark theme (light only for print/export); EOC console simplified and centred on the ICS Canada 209 | Owner brief (redesign note 2026-10-06); dark tokens `f0a3c28` (PR #15), map-first layout `d8affaa` (PR #16). 209 rebuilt on ICS Canada Form 209-WF `d34b349` (2026-10-08, below) |
| 2026-10-06 | FireSim must never suggest evacuation tiers; Planning sets Order / Alert / Watch, drawn as blue outlines | Owner decision; implemented `c815fcf` (PR #20, merged 2026-10-07) |
| 2026-10-06 | Times in America/Edmonton; Alberta permanent UTC-6 from 2026-06-18 (IANA tzdata 2026c); old tz data shows MST after 2026-11-01 | `89a0a4b`; `docs/model-card.md` |
| 2026-10-07 | Positioning: preparedness/training tool now; Prometheus-level real-time accuracy is the later goal. Build order: validation harness → ensemble-first product → fixed-horizon 209 snapshots → time-available vs time-needed → trigger buffers / ember reach → exercise/replay | Owner, after the internal EOC best-practice review [R1]. Review's "avoid" list stands: no evac tier suggestions, no headline single arrival time, no per-building loss probability, no barrier credit for rivers/roads |
| 2026-10-07 | OSM water mask off by default; rebuild water from authoritative hydrography | OSM layer (2,275 polygons, 429 invalid) masked 17-21 % of C-2/D-2/M-2 cells; LiDAR grid already maps water as non-fuel (`d5f3bc1`, PR #23; internal fuel-grid report [R2]) |
| 2026-10-07 | Measure every prediction improvement on the CFSDS validation harness (Bennett et al. 2026 protocol), held-out fires for settings | `498fec7` (PR #22), `37311ef`/`6934822` (PR #27) |
| 2026-10-08 | UI defaults = held-out validated set-up (burning period 10-20 h, FFMC spin-up with hourly forecast, active edges when marked); API defaults stay off | `c35e308`, `c53fafb` (PR #28); `docs/model-card.md` |
| 2026-10-08 | Critical assets and roads are automatic from open sources (no user JSON); report arrival times (single run + ensemble P10) per asset instead of a 50 %-burn-probability flag | Owner decision 2026-10-08; `0751583` (PR #29) |
| 2026-10-08 | ~~The EOC location may be published (manual point in `assets.geojson`)~~ **Superseded** the same day (below: EOC point left out) | Owner, 2026-10-08 (roadmap note); `docs/data-sources.md` |
| 2026-10-08 | Critical-asset data gaps closed: care facilities from the current Government of Alberta continuing care list (geocoded with City address points) + ODHF + OSM, merged by name within 150 m with every source kept per feature; ODHF-only sites flagged "possibly closed", OSM-only "verify" (11 → 127 sites); EOC kept but marked "Unverified manual point" everywhere (no public source gives its address; superseded by the next row); roads rebuilt from OSM incl. `secondary` and ramps by a kept script; generic same-name de-duplication within 300 m (30 merged); burn-probability area table labelled model output | `2357a02` (PR #31, merged `03997fd`); `docs/data-sources.md` |
| 2026-10-08 | The EOC point is left out of the critical-assets layer: no public source gives the EOC's location and the old manual point was unverifiable (supersedes "The EOC location may be published") | Owner decision 2026-10-08; PR #33 (`8da78f5`, merged `dd523f6`); `assets.geojson` 623 features |
| 2026-10-08 | Situation report = ICS Canada Form 209-WF (May 2021), not NIMS ICS-209. Model-filled blocks tagged MODEL OUTPUT (7, 27, 29, 30B, 36, 38, 42); observed blocks 9 (status) and 28 (observed behaviour) user-entered only; projections at 12/24/48/72 h in clock time from the ensemble (P50, P10) where available; run ID + model version stamped; unsourced HFI→complexity mapping removed; other ICS forms cite their ICS Canada counterparts (layouts are adaptations) | Owner choice (ICS Canada); form checked against icscanada.ca PDF; `d34b349`; `docs/ics-canada-209.md` |
| 2026-10-08 | Engine runs are repeatable: spotting draws from a private `random.Random` seeded by `SimulationConfig.seed` (API `seed`) or a SHA-256 of the other inputs; no global random state | `f681189`; `docs/verification.md` §5 |
| 2026-10-08 | Real-raster integration tests start from known O-1a fuel cells of the reprojected grid and must run, not skip | `af052e4` |
| 2026-10-09 | Grid model: point ignitions must burn the same area whatever the wind's angle to the grid. Fixed numerically, FBP physics unchanged: the starting ellipse is connected through cell corners (not across diagonal non-fuel, not through zero-ROS fuel), the level set uses a rotated upwind stencil (grid axis + grid diagonal bracketing the Huygens velocity) instead of axis-by-axis upwinding, and phi starts as the signed distance to the starting ellipse. Rotational invariance is now a test (O-1a within 12 %, C-2 within 5 % over six wind directions; head within 10° of downwind) | Grass point ignition 43 cells at 180° vs 3 at 225°; ablation in `docs/verification.md` §2. `4bcfb1d` (PR #35). Validation effect: §4.4 |
| 2026-10-09 | Ensemble perturbation sizes = Alberta input-error climatology x one inflation factor (1.5) chosen on the calibration fires (wind dir 24°, ws log 0.405, FFMC 7.2, DMC/DC log 0.6, ROS log 0.825, FMC 15; curing 13.5 not inflated). "Worst-credible" dropped for P10 everywhere (API, docs, UI): P10 = reached by 1 in 10 members, the early end of the modelled range | Held-out ensemble calibration: coverage 20 → 49 %, CRPS skill vs single run +0.11 → +0.24, P10 held ≥ 90 % of observed growth on only 29 % of days (`docs/validation.md` "Ensemble calibration"; PR feat/ensemble-calibration) |
| 2026-10-09 | Rebuild the Berkeley/UMD WUI structure-spread model (Purnomo, Qin, Trouvé, Gollner et al.; the ELMFIRE WUI extensions) **from the published literature only**, as an opt-in, illustrative layer: specification first, then a per-building unit layer, then the Hamada option; WU-E, embers and validation later | Owner decision 2026-10-09 after the structure-ignition paper check [R4]. Licence: ELMFIRE is AGPL v3 + Commons Clause, so its source is never opened, read, quoted or translated; every equation and constant is cited to a paper, section, equation and page (`docs/structure-spread-spec.md`) |
| 2026-10-09 | Buildings are single units (graph nodes from the Microsoft footprints) coupled to the FBP grid front, not cells of the fuel grid | Qin et al. (2026) FSJ 104686 pp.7-9 [37]: structure spread converges only with cells ≤ half the structure size and separation; Edmonton side yards would need 1-3 m cells (`docs/structure-spread-spec.md` §2) |
| 2026-10-09 | Structure-spread defaults where the papers conflict (spec §7): design fire 150 kW/m² (PROCI) with 400 kW/m² as a scenario (~~owner to confirm~~ confirmed, D1 below); radiant fraction 0.3; PROCI SI flame-reach ellipse behind a verification gate; ember delay τ = 42 s; Hamada c₃ on V²; IJWF a = downwind; Hamada T in minutes; neighbour cutoff 30 m (heuristic, ~~owner to confirm~~ confirmed, D2 below) | `docs/structure-spread-spec.md` §4.4, §7 |
| 2026-10-09 | **D1** House design fire: 150 kW/m² default (5 min growth, 1 min full, 60 min decay; PROCI24 after NIST TN 1600), 400 kW/m² as a named "heavier fuel load" scenario; spec C1 stands. No current output changes (used only by the unbuilt WU-E stage) | Owner delegated the six structure-spread questions on 2026-10-09 ("trust your judgement… documented with reasoning"); decisions report [R6]. 150 is the only value used in a run compared with building-loss data (PROCI24, FSJ104651); a training tool should not default to the more severe value without evidence |
| 2026-10-09 | **D2** Keep `neighbour_cutoff_m` 30 m and `wildland_contact_m` 10 m; measure the sensitivity (cutoff 20 / 30 / 45 m, contact 5 / 10 / 20 m) and change a default only if a result is unstable or unphysical | [R6]. Evidence anchors: IJWF24 Fig. 7a and NRC (2021) p.27 for 30 m; Cohen (2000) flame-contact band for 10 m; no Canadian loss data to choose on. Sensitivity [R7]: cutoff −48 % to +220 %, contact −71 % to +557 % (involved buildings at 6 h); defaults kept; contact found grid-dependent (open item, §6) |
| 2026-10-09 | **D3** (superseded 2026-10-10, below) Structure-spread output stays aggregate (per-frame counts; later by neighbourhood or distance band). Per-building involvement times are not returned in UI, API or exports; revisit only after a Canadian validation | [R6]. 2026-10-07 avoid-list [R1] rules out per-building loss outputs; building-level precision in FSJ104651 was 9-77 %; a per-house map would read as a loss forecast |
| 2026-10-09 | **D4** Keep all 346,238 Microsoft footprints (garages and sheds) by default; test a < 40 m² size filter [H] instead of a City parcel cross-check (parcel join deferred) | [R6]. Outbuildings burn; removing them widens gaps and slows spread (less cautious). Result [R7]: the filter changes involved buildings by −8 % to 0 %, and the city-wide median nearest separation stays 2.6 m without footprints < 40 m² (short gaps are mostly not small sheds) |
| 2026-10-09 | **D5** Jasper 2024 building-level data: search the public record first (Parks Canada, Municipality of Jasper, provincial/federal after-action reviews, published damage inspections); only if nothing usable is public does the owner ask through the EM network. Validation (spec §8) stays unexecuted until then | [R6]. Jasper is the only recent, well-documented Canadian WUI loss event close to FireSim's setting; public data are reproducible and citable |
| 2026-10-09 | **D6** Hamada rate: keep the published equations (0.197 m/s at a₀ = d = 10 m, f_b = 1, 17.8 m/s) against Qin (2025)'s 0.34 m/s; keep the strict xfail and the open item; **no contact with the authors** (owner instruction) | [R6]; spec §4.5, C11. Wind height, units and the Hazus blend above 10 m/s were checked and do not explain 0.34; matching it would add an unsourced factor of ~1.7 |

| 2026-10-10 | **Ensemble empty-member fix and partial-failure rule.** Production (Fly, simulation 70fa2753, Edmonton 4 h grid run): "The ensemble did not finish (index 0 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 0)". Root cause: the ignition hold in `run_cellular_simulation` accepted a head ROS > 1e-6 m/min, the starting ellipse (`_initial_front`) required > 1e-5; a rate between them gave a starting fire with no burned cell and `_window` indexed an empty mask. How members got there: FBP (cffdrs) floors a zero ROS at 1e-6 ("no spread", e.g. D-2 green aspen below BUI 80); the member's DMC/DC perturbation took BUI below 80 at a D-2 ignition and its ROS multiplier (log-normal, median 1) scaled the floor to 1e-6..1e-5. Members with multiplier ≤ 1 held forever (0 cells, the logged "0 cells burned"), > 1 crashed, > 10 burned the ignition cell spuriously. The deterministic run (BUI above 80, multiplier 1) spread. Evidence: D-2 grid, BUI 87, 30 members seed 1, old engine 7 zero / 6 IndexError / 17 burning, each crash exactly a BUI < 80 member with multiplier > 1; the same members on the Edmonton grid (ignition in a D-2 stand) reproduce the production error via the API. Fix: one threshold `CARRY_ROS` = 1e-5 for hold and ellipse; FBP's floored ROS is set to 0 before any multiplier (ensemble ROS error or WUI modifier), as it means no spread, not a rate; `_window` returns empty slices for an empty mask and a starting fire without a cell is treated as no fire (a zero-burn run returns frames with 0 ha). Ensemble: each member runs in a try/except (logged with member index, seed and perturbation); percentiles, burn probability and area range are over the finished members, **zero-burn members included** (they are in the burn-probability denominator); the ensemble fails only when **fewer than 50 %** of members finish (`MIN_OK_FRACTION`; below that the statistics would rest on few, possibly unrepresentative members, since failures depend on the perturbation). API adds `members_ok`, `members_failed`, `failed`; the card shows "k of N members failed and are left out". Not fixed (open item §6, seasonal fuel switch): the Edmonton LiDAR grid labels aspen D-2 year-round, so in leaf-off months the D-2 BUI-80 switch applies where D-1 would spread | Owner bug report 2026-10-10; `engine/tests/spread/test_ensemble_robustness.py`, API `test_ensemble_*` |

Explicit values are always used (backward compatible for clients that send one) | Owner delegated the mechanics choices on 2026-10-10 ("go with your views on the mechanics"); decisions report [R12], evidence [R13] rec 1. | 2026-10-10 | **Starting FWI codes come from Pyra's chain** (owner goal: "the fire-weather product and the prediction start from the same numbers"). `/api/v1/weather/current` asks a "pyra" tier first: for a point in Alberta it takes the nearest station in Pyra's list and reproduces Pyra's station page (`initFWI`, Pyra `38aee4f`): carry-over = the newer of the CWFIS current-layer pick (Pyra's holding cache) and Pyra's published `data/cwfis_prev.json` entry, ≤ 2 days old; before noon LST stepped one day with the GEM noon-LST forecast (the same Open-Meteo URL Pyra requests); after noon CWFIS codes as published, else stepped with a weather-only CWFIS observation or GEM noon. FireSim's own calculator does the step (same equations, 147.2). Labels follow Pyra ("Noon LST Forecast · today (pre-noon)"). Pyra's rules are copied as they are, including the ones this record would not choose (one step from a 2-day-old carry-over, [R15] M13; the spring DC floor heuristic; a co-located station's codes when the CWFIS order puts it first), because the point is the same number, not a better one; differences are listed in §6 for the Pyra session. Fallback: CWFIS → archive → GEM cold start (labelled), with the reason the Pyra tier was not used appended. User-entered codes still win. `FIRESIM_PYRA_SOURCE=0` turns the tier off | Why consume Pyra's inputs rather than its numbers: Pyra publishes no "today" codes; they are computed in the browser from the carry-over file and a live GEM request, so FireSim must repeat that step. Why server-side and cached: one cached fetch of a ~155 KB file per 15 min (raw.githubusercontent.com CDN ~5 min, file changes once a day at 20:15 UTC); the last good copy is used on failure up to Pyra's 48 h limit, then GitHub Pages, then the old chain. Why a copy of Pyra's station list (`services/pyra_stations.py`, exported by `scripts/pyra/export_pyra_stations.mjs`): Pyra publishes it only inside `fwi.js`; a check against a Pyra checkout runs when `PYRA_DIR` is set. `source_tier` (not `source`) carries the machine-readable "pyra" because `source` is already the display string the UI shows. Parity: §5 |
| 2026-10-10 | **Building burn-out (structure spread step 4; spec §4.3, C21).** Every involved unit burns the building design fire and is burnt out when it ends: 66 min (150 kW/m², PROCI24 p.3) or 70 min (400 kW/m², FSJ104686 p.2) after involvement; the end of the curve is the end of combustion (PROCI24 p.2; Qin25 eq 6.2 p.146). A burnt-out unit passes no fire: a Hamada crossing not complete by then never arrives [H] and ember emission stops (as before, Qin25 p.67). | 2026-10-11 | **Bug: re-running showed the old run (single with ensemble → multi-day).** Owner: after a quick single run with the ensemble on, switching Setup → Run options to Multi-day and running again still showed the old 4 h run while the app polled `GET /simulations/db74ce05/ensemble` every ~2 s for minutes; clearing first made the multi-day run work. Investigation: the code on `8743ad5` (= the production deployment) resets the frames and the ensemble key on every start, and a Playwright reproduction of exactly that flow against the mocked API passed on `8743ad5` (multi-day request sent, old poll stopped, 72 h timeline) — **the stale view did not reproduce**. What the reproduction cannot show is the server side: the old run's ensemble kept computing in the API process (it is never stopped; a 30-member Terwillegar 4 h ensemble took 515 s on the workstation), so on the 1 shared vCPU the new multi-day run had no frames for minutes and the old one stayed in view. **Fix:** (a) one `leaveCurrentRun` path for every new run (single, multi-day, perimeter restart) and Clear results: the ensemble poll stops and the app sends `DELETE /simulations/{id}/ensemble`, which stops the ensemble before its next member (`run_ensemble(should_stop=…)`, status `cancelled`); (b) a single run with the hourly forecast clears the previous run as soon as Run is pressed (`prepareRun`), not after the forecast fetch; (c) WebSocket events for another simulation ID are ignored (late POST responses already were, by run token); (d) `useEnsemble` treats `cancelled` as off. Not done: the server does not stop an abandoned ensemble on its own (a client that never sends DELETE, e.g. a closed tab, still leaves it running) | Owner report 2026-10-11; `frontend/src/hooks/rerun.test.ts`, `frontend/tests/e2e/rerun.spec.ts`, API `test_cancel_*`, engine `test_should_stop_cancels_before_the_next_member` |
| 2026-10-11 | **Bug: multi-day runs very slow (live: 2 days, uniform C-2, 120 min snapshots, burning 10-20 sat at hour 2 of 48 for minutes).** Profiled locally (engine only, `_execute_multiday`): the same request takes 11.7 s on the workstation, so the engine alone does not explain minutes; contention does (above: the previous run's ensemble in the same 1-vCPU process; locally a concurrent ensemble slowed the run only 1.2x, but on the shared Fly CPU it competes for the whole burst budget — not measured on Fly). Engine cost: 70 % in `simplify_front`: an O(n²) angular resample (a `min()` scan with `atan2` for every target angle over every hull vertex, ~14.5 M calls for 2 days), a redundant angle sort of every wavelet point (~7,600 points a step), and per-vertex FBP and trigonometry for wavelets that are identical in uniform fuel. **Fix (results unchanged):** bisection over precomputed angles with `min()`'s tie-breaking, the hull loop on plain floats, one wavelet per distinct (fuel, slope, aspect, modifier, canopy) per step. Frames are bit-identical on 8 configs (multi-day C-2 with/without DEM, D-1 3 days no burning period, O-1b, Edmonton grid 3 days; single C-2 4 h, M-1 8 h, O-1b 6 h); times: 2-day C-2 11.7 → 5.7 s, with DEM 9.7 → 5.6 s, 3-day D-1 33.8 → 17.9 s, O-1b 13.8 → 7.5 s, single C-2 4 h 3.5 → 1.4 s. No burning-period wait loop or snapshot problem in the Huygens path (no-spread periods step at 5 min with zero distance, cheap); the grid path already jumps to the next weather change. **Memory:** the DEM's slope/aspect were lists of Python floats, ~560 MB for the Edmonton DEM (4.8 M cells) held in the grid cache for the process lifetime; now `array('d')` rows (~76 MB, same values). **Edmonton grid, 3 days, burning 10-20:** 21 s locally (CFL-limited level-set steps, unchanged), peak RSS 713 → 425 MB; without burning period 76 s, 361 MB. Frames streamed: multi-day accepts `cells_mode: "incremental"` (whole frame at each day start), 21.5 → 1.65 MB for the 3-day run | Owner report 2026-10-11; [R20]; engine `test_hull_and_resampling_match_the_reference`, `test_front_expansion_shares_wavelets_without_changing_them`, `test_float_rows_are_compact_and_exact`; API `test_multiday_incremental_frames_rebuild_cumulative` |

| 2026-10-11 | **Buildings for all of Alberta, read by window** (owner-approved task). (1) Data: Microsoft Canadian Building Footprints, Alberta (2019 release; the Edmonton extract is exactly its footprints with ring-average centroid in 53.39-53.71 N, 113.75-113.25 W, same coordinates), one FlatGeobuf with a packed Hilbert R-tree, fields `id` (Edmonton ids kept, others 1,000,000 + source position) and `area_m2`; no address fields. FlatGeobuf over GeoParquet: box reads through the R-tree touch only the matching features with no in-memory state, pyogrio (bundled GDAL) reads it locally or via `/vsicurl/`; GeoParquet would need pyarrow and decodes whole row groups. (2) Statistics Canada ODB v3 evaluated and **not used**: in five municipal windows 94-99 % of Microsoft footprints are in ODB, ODB adds 5-37 % (mostly < 20 m² accessory buildings and newer houses), but it covers only 11 municipalities (+ some federal footprints), carries per-municipality licences and address fields, and mixing it would change the building unit in some towns and break Edmonton equivalence; a per-municipality switch is an owner decision. (3) Engine/API: `BuildingIndex` keeps the Edmonton 4-nearest-neighbourhood selection exactly (former extract box; windows = neighbourhood polygons + Voronoi cells), structure spread reads the reachable box (clipped to the extract box for Edmonton runs), runs elsewhere use the buildings in the run grid box (≤ `FIRESIM_BUILDINGS_MAX_NEAR` 30,000 nearest); the request path `edmonton_buildings.geojson.gz` is resolved to the Alberta file when installed. (4) Community units outside Edmonton: StatCan 2021 census subdivisions (named places), not dissemination areas (unnamed codes). (5) Vegetation around buildings outside Edmonton stays on demand (`build_building_vegetation.py ring-metrics` per area), not province-wide | Owner task 2026-10-11. Equivalence and memory measured against `master` `1b377b9` [R25]; `docs/buildings-alberta.md` |

| 2026-10-11 | **Run archive, phase 1 (storage option A).** Design as proposed in [R28] §6 with these choices: (1) the archive stores the **serialised live GET body** (plus `/arrival`, `/ensemble` bodies) rather than engine frames, so an archived GET is shape-identical by construction (incl. multi-day `day`/`day_fwi` and incremental `cells_offset`) and needs no second serialiser; (2) archiving is hooked on the **end of the run thread**, not on the status change, because a run is "completed" before its ensemble finishes; one worker thread, failures logged only; failed runs are archived too (audit), paused-forever runs are not; (3) payloads are read from the object store even without an index row, and `meta.json` copies the row, so a lost index loses no run; (4) the local backend is the default (tests, development, Fly without a volume: temp dir + warning); S3 via boto3 as an optional extra, falling back to local without credentials; (5) Litestream 0.5.17 in the image, used only when `FIRESIM_ARCHIVE_DB` and the R2 secrets exist, with a config check and a bounded (120 s) restore so it can never stop the API; (6) per-building fields kept exactly as the live GET returns them (centroid exposure, illustrative structure polygons; no addresses), not stripped, so a reloaded run displays the same; no export or 209 reads the archive; (7) no deploy, volume, bucket or secret created (owner approval). | Owner approval of option A phase 1, 2026-10-11; [R28] §6; [R30]; tests §5 |

| 2026-10-12 | **Alberta grid grass and 105 decisions; licence request recommended** (owner: "do the research, then make the decision and document it"). (1) Code 31 O-1 → **O-1b all year**, no date rule: ST-X-3 describes matted grass as "common after snowmelt or in the spring" (p.16), i.e. a fuel state; neither the grid nor the CWFIS curing grids record it; O-1b ≥ O-1a from ISI 8 (×1.05 at 10, ×1.21 at 20, ×1.33 at 60; O-1a ≤ ~1 m/min faster below) and the ratio is independent of curing; O-1b at 100 % curing reproduced a snow-free southern Alberta grass/cropland run (Alexander et al. 2013, p.26); a date swap needs per-source labels because the City grid's O-1a/O-1b share them. (2) Code 105 → **non-fuel kept**, not made configurable (needs per-cell source codes after stacking): 105 is cropland and recently burned land, matching Alberta's and Prometheus' non-fuel coding; limitation: cured stubble and burn regrowth can carry fire in spring/fall. `PREREG_105` (pushed `a61ac7e` before scoring) was void: 25 of 29 fires of 2025 ≥ 1,000 ha failed the map-timing gate. (3) **Ask** the Wildfire Geospatial Intelligence Unit for written licence confirmation and build rules: the grid is redistributed in a public release from an item that carries only the GoA disclaimer. | [R35]; `docs/fuel-grid-alberta.md` §3, §9 |

| 2026-10-12 | **Edmonton extract box: dropped as a limit on buildings, kept as the Edmonton-ignition test, fallback-assignment partition and Microsoft-only zone** (owner delegated the decision). Structure spread in Edmonton runs reads every building the fire can reach; buildings outside the box within a City neighbourhood polygon are assigned to it (polygon only; the nearest-centroid fallback stays inside the box). Reasons: the box is an artefact of how the old extract was cut; it hid 8,325 buildings from City-grid runs (units in run 334,213 → 342,538) incl. Edmonton's own southern neighbourhoods, Nisku and the airport; south/east-edge runs undercounted involvement up to 27× (Walker 7 → 189, Cavanagh 19 → 61, south-east 4 → 44, east edge 171 → 247); inconsistent with the rest of Alberta; no validation depends on it (Edmonton sensitivity reads the GeoJSON without a box; Jasper uses its own damage footprints; CFSDS has no buildings). Runs that do not reach the edge are unchanged (15 of 19 selections, 6 of 10 runs identical). `FIRESIM_EDMONTON_BOX_LEGACY=1` restores the former behaviour for comparisons | [R34] §6; `docs/buildings-alberta.md` §6 |
| 2026-10-12 | **City of Edmonton LiDAR fuel grid stays; it is open data (OGL – Edmonton).** A task to replace it with the open-data grid (it was recorded as City-internal, §6) was stopped when the owner found the 2025 LiDAR on data.edmonton.ca (`sz3i-y5ze`, 2025 Lidar – Full Feature, flown 29-30 June 2025, published 2026-06-12, "See Terms of Use" = OGL – City of Edmonton v1.0). Licence check ([R38] §1): build `edmonton-burnp3` `223dd29` `scripts/build_fuel_raster.py` reads crowns and canopy from the 2025 LiDAR tiles, uPLVI 2025 (`gm9t-5epd`) and Naturalized Areas (`ct88-r8tk`); its crown classifier (Random Forest, 45 LiDAR features) was trained on the City Trees inventory (`eecg-fc54`, 474,289 trees), all OGL – Edmonton; no 2019 LiDAR and no internal tree table. The grid stays in the repo and image, credited in About; [R21]'s "internal" is corrected to the 2019 contract. Residual: the build used a vegetation-classified LAZ copy while the portal item is described as GeoTIFF (confirm with City GIS, §6). The open-data grid v2.1 is not adopted; its comparison and provenance (stand objects cut with a City-LiDAR-trained conifer model) are recorded in [R38] | Owner decision 2026-10-12 (coordinator relay); data.edmonton.ca metadata read 2026-10-12; [R38] |

Applies to every source (Microsoft and ODB), to structure-spread counts and to exposure / inside-perimeter counts; outbuildings stay in the unit graph, the building mask and the map detail (`small: true`). 20 m², not 40: the ODB / Microsoft count ratio per town falls between 0 and 20 m² (Lethbridge 1.85 → 1.32, Grande Prairie 1.72 → 1.25, Canmore 1.41 → 1.19) and is flat from 20 to 60 m² except rural Parkland County; 40 m² would cut detached garages both sources map (DINS Eaton minor structures median 55 m²). Counted separately, not dropped: sheds burn and bridge gaps (Grande Prairie edge run 251 counted with them in the graph vs 195 without), and DINS itself inspects structures ≥ 120 ft² but reports "Other Minor Structure" apart. Edmonton changes only where an outbuilding is involved or within a band (live request 43 → 42 involved, 1,006 → 991 within 500 m; Terwillegar identical), accepted for one unit definition everywhere; `FIRESIM_SMALL_FOOTPRINT_M2=0` restores the former counts exactly | [R40] §2-3; spec §2.2; `docs/buildings-alberta.md` §5 |

| 2026-10-12 | **Perimeter restart uses Setup's weather; Predict N h runs N h** (owner field tests [R41] bugs 1-2, [R42] §3, §7; owner-approved item A). (1) POST perimeter-override takes optional `weather`, `fwi_overrides`, `weather_schedule`; the app sends Setup's current values (the schedule step in force at the restart time, later steps re-based); omitted → the source run's weather and schedule re-based (`weather_used.source` = `source_run`). The restart runs as a normal run of its effective request (`restart_config`), so GET `config`, `weather_used`, the header chips and the archive show what it used. (2) The "cap" (Predict 4 h → 1 h / 2 h, "Run complete") and runs one frame short were **not** an engine limit: the server computed every frame (live restart `aff7c0b4`: GET 9 frames to 4.0 h, WebSocket delivered 7 to 3.0 h). Each frame was sent by its own task; on a slow link the handler sent `simulation.completed` and closed the socket while the last, largest frames were still in flight. Fix: the handler is the socket's only writer, sends frames in order from the run, then `completed` with `n_frames`; the app re-reads the run if any is missing. Continuous run vs a run restarted from its own mid-run perimeter, identical weather. Engine, uniform fuel: +1.1 % (C-2, 20 km/h), +2.2 % (C-2, 47), +1.8 % (O-1b); mixed synthetic grid +6.8 %; head ROS identical. Flanks and back are not treated as heads (the level set moves every edge at its FBP ellipse rate in both runs) and an established fire has no acceleration to lose. Slave Lake grid, 47 km/h, 6 h continuous 496.5 ha: restart from its 2 h perimeter **997.5 ha before, 598.8 ha after** the fix (the continuous head stalls at Poplar Estates' building cells, the unmasked restart burned through); from 2.5-4 h +10 %. Residual: the restarted front is rebuilt at burned-cell edges (a perimeter carries no sub-cell position), up to half a cell ahead where the continuous front was held at a fuel boundary or gap. Setup → Weather & FWI table (time, wind, direction, temperature, RH, rain mm/h) applied as step changes; API `weather_schedule` (hours after `start_time`); `firesim.spread.diurnal.schedule_hourly` turns it into hourly records (every whole hour and every step), so the existing `hourly_weather` machinery recomputes FFMC hourly from the starting code with the hourly FFMC model (ISI follows), the ensemble perturbs every step, and restarts get the step in force and the later ones. Not with hourly forecast weather or the spin-up. Shown in `weather_used`, the archive request JSON, the ICS 209 weather block, ICS text, GeoJSON/KML metadata and EOC conditions. Consequence: a schedule run's FFMC drifts with its weather (20 °C, RH 15 %: 90 → 91.8 after 2 h, 93.5 after 6 h), unlike a constant-weather run that keeps the daily FFMC | Van Wagner (1977) hourly FFMC as already used for forecast weather; `engine/tests/spread/test_weather_schedule.py` |

(2) In a multi-day run day-1 arrival times carry "D1" like the later days ("D1 10:00" beside "D2 12:15"); single-day runs are unchanged. | 2026-10-18 | **Round-4 field test fixes: ICS 209 freeze, reload re-attach, day on the clock** (Claude in Chrome tester on the live #86 build, [R56]; headless Playwright reproduction with a mocked 145-frame 72 h run). (1) ICS 209 froze the tab: not per-frame work (≈0.6 s main thread at 145 frames). Two causes: every ICS form waited for the map's `render` event, which never fires while the page draws no frames (locked screen, hidden or occluded window: reproduced, ICS-201 never opened), and the 209 opened a pop-up that called `window.print()` on load, whose print dialog blocks the app's tab (shared event loop) until answered (inferred; headless shows the call, not the dialog). Now the 209 is previewed in the console like the other forms, no form prints by itself (Print button in its own window), the snapshot waits ≤ 1.5 s and not at all on a hidden page, and the console map keeps its drawing buffer (MapLibre 5 `canvasContextAttributes`; the former top-level `preserveDrawingBuffer` was ignored), which also embeds the map on form open (round-3 R5). (2) The run on screen is kept in sessionStorage (this tab only) and re-read by ID after a reload (GET `/simulations/{id}`, followed to completion; saved-runs auth unchanged). (3) Clocks (timeline, ticks, Situation) carry the calendar day like the map labels ("D2 03:32"; "D1" in multi-day runs); the elapsed keeps the operational day, so a 72 h run's last frame reads "D4 04:57 MDT · D3 · T+72:00". (4) Time left from the last 2 min of progress, hedged. (5) About: a 3-day run takes about 1-15 min. (6) Grid runs: "Fuel grid, burned: O1a 58 %, O1b 32 % …". (7) Station notice old → new. (8) Clear results clears the run chips. Round-3 R1: type-ahead neighbourhood picker | `EOCConsole.tsx`, `utils/ics209.ts` (`runFuelLabel`, `modelVersionWithin`), `MapView.tsx`, `utils/liveRun.ts`, `useSimulation.restoreRun`, `TimeSlider.tsx`, `SituationPanel.tsx`, `RunProgress.tsx` `etaFromSamples`, `WeatherPanel.tsx` `weatherChangeBrief`, `EvacStatusPanel.tsx`, `content/explanations.ts` |
| 2026-10-18 | **Alberta's permanent UTC-6 clock: dated rule for the wall clock, fire-weather day unchanged** (backlog §F; owner-approved 2026-10-18, label "MT" chosen by the owner). Verified rule: Alberta's Official Time Act puts the province on UTC-6 all year; in force 2026-06-18; IANA tzdata 2026c models it as no fall-back on 2026-11-01 02:00 MDT (abbreviation CST). The "returns to MST" note is wrong. (1) Wall clock: `frontend/src/utils/time.ts` and `api/.../services/alberta_time.py` use the tz data before 2026-11-01 08:00 UTC and fixed UTC-6 from then, whatever the browser's or server's tz data says (Node 22 ICU 2025b still falls back). Labels MST/MDT before the change (historical replays such as Slave Lake 2011 keep MDT), "MT" after (never the Intl abbreviation). (2) Fire-weather day: the CFFDRS noon-LST observation for Alberta stays at **19 UTC (13:00 MT)**: CWFIS gives every Alberta station a fixed UTC-LST correction of -7 "estimated from coordinate ranges and maps" (Saskatchewan, legally UTC-6 for decades, is split -6/-7 by longitude), Alberta Wildfire defines its PM Actual in MST, and CWFIS daily values match the ECCC 19 UTC hourly record (Edmonton Int'l CS, 17 Oct 2026). No agency has announced a change after 1 Nov, so the offset for CFFDRS days from 2026-11-01 is one setting, `FIRESIM_AB_FWI_LST_OFFSET_H` (7 default; 6 = 18 UTC); days before are always 19 UTC. Peak-burn hour stays 22 UTC (16:00 on the UTC-6 clock). (3) tz data: API depends on `tzdata>=2026.3` and the image reads it (`PYTHONTZPATH=""`). Pyra switched together (companion PR). | [R57]; open sources only, no agency contacted |
| 2026-10-18 | **Noon LST per CWFIS station where a province has more than one value** (owner follow-up to [R57], approved in principle 2026-10-18). CWFIS's `tz_correct` is a fixed per-station UTC-LST correction, not the legal clock: Saskatchewan is -6 in the south and east (96 stations) and -7 in the north-west (38: Waskesiu, Buffalo Narrows, La Loche, Meadow Lake Park, …), BC -8 except 75 eastern Mountain-time stations at -7, and Ontario, Quebec, Newfoundland and Labrador and Nunavut are mixed. The boundary is irregular (Meadow Lake A -6, Meadow Lake Park -7, 30 km apart), so no longitude rule fits: `routers/weather.py` takes the value of the nearest CWFIS station of the same province from the 2026 table (`services/cwfis_tz.py`, `cwfis_tz_data.py` generated by `scripts/build_cwfis_tz.py`, SHA-256 recorded) for the codes-age label (the reporting station's own coordinates) and the GEM noon hour (the point). Province table corrected to CWFIS: CWFIS codes `SA`/`NF` accepted (the table's `SK`/`NL` never matched CWFIS records), Yukon -8, Newfoundland whole hours (-3/-4). | ECCC hourly match 17 Oct 2026: Saskatoon, Regina 18 UTC; Waskesiu, Buffalo Narrows 19 UTC; Kamloops, Prince George 20 UTC; Cranbrook, Dawson Creek 19 UTC ([R57] addendum) |
