## 4. Methods and references

### 4.1 Method → source

| Component | Implementation | Source |
|---|---|---|
| FBP fuel types, ROS, BUI effect, SFC, CFB, TFC, HFI, LB, back/flank ROS, net effective wind + slope, FMC from date, point-ignition acceleration | `fbp/calculator.py`, `fbp/constants.py`, `fbp/crown_fire.py` | ST-X-3 [1] eqs 1-8, 26-58, 70-73, 81; GLC-X-10 [2] (grass curing eqs 35a/b, M-4 `c`, revised forms); verified against `cffdrs` [6] |
| D-2 no spread below BUI 80 | `fbp/calculator.py` | Alexander (2010) [7] **as cited by cffdrs**; full reference from the reference lists of de Groot et al. (2022) and CFS NOR-X-433 (2026-10-10); the paper itself not read |
| Crown fire initiation, CFB | `fbp/crown_fire.py` | Van Wagner (1977) crown fire [3]; ST-X-3 eqs 56-58. Context only (not implemented): the physically based CFIM of Cruz et al. (2006) [50, 51] finds flame-zone depth and the fuel strata gap dominate and FMC matters much less than Van Wagner assumes (Part II pp.61, 70) |
| FWI System (FFMC, DMC, DC, ISI, BUI, FWI) | `fwi/calculator.py` | Van Wagner & Pickett (1985) [5]; Van Wagner (1987) [5b]: FFMC scale eqs 2a/2b (printed p. 4, PDF p. 15); DMC scale eq 16 (printed p. 10, PDF p. 21) and rain eqs 17-19 (printed p. 11); DC eqs 22-26 (printed pp. 13-14, PDF pp. 24-25) with Lf Table 3 (printed p. 15). Cold-day DC floor T ≥ −2.8 °C as in cffdrs [6] (from 2026-10-10). Verified against cffdrs on the 48-day test sequence + 21 edge cases (`engine/tests/fwi/test_cffdrs_fwi_reference.py`) |
| Starting codes from Pyra (Alberta, tier 1 of current weather) | `api/.../services/pyra.py`, `services/pyra_stations.py`, `routers/weather.py` | Pyra [55] station-page chain (`initFWI`, `_selectCWFIS`, `_carryOverFor`, `fetchWeather`, `applyDCFloor`; commit `38aee4f`) reproduced with FireSim's FWI calculator (Van Wagner 1987 [5b]: daily codes from noon-LST weather, PDF p. 13); inputs: Pyra's `cwfis_prev.json` (CWFIS mirror), CWFIS WFS, Open-Meteo GEM [46]. Spring DC floor = Pyra heuristic ("calibrated Apr 2026", unsourced) |
| Current fire weather / codes (tiers 2-3) | `api/.../routers/weather.py` | CWFIS WFS `public:firewx_stns_current`, fallback `public:firewx_stns` (newest day ≤ 2 days old); nearest station with codes; noon LST from the province's standard offset; off-season cold start 85/6/15 (Van Wagner 1987 [5b]; VW&P 1985 [5]) with Open-Meteo GEM [46] noon weather + 24 h rain |
| Forecast weather | `frontend/src/services/api.ts` (`FORECAST_MODEL`), API estimate | Open-Meteo forecast API [46], model `gem_seamless` (ECCC GEM blend; Open-Meteo GEM API docs). Pinned 2026-10-10; before that `best_match` |
| Hourly FFMC | `fwi/` hourly path | Van Wagner (1977) PS-X-69 [4]; matches cffdrs `hffmc` |
| Diurnal burning: FFMC spin-up from 17:00 local the previous day; burning period (spread only between set hours) | `spread/diurnal.py` | Lawson et al. (1996) [17] (daily FFMC ≈ 16:00 LST), Beck et al. (2002) [16], burning conditions as in Prometheus, Tymstra et al. (2010) [10]. 10-20 h chosen on calibration fires (§4.4) |
| Manual weather schedule (step changes during a run) | `spread/diurnal.py` `schedule_hourly` → `SimulationConfig.hourly_weather`; API `weather_schedule`; UI `utils/weatherSchedule.ts` | Hourly FFMC as above (Van Wagner 1977 [4]), recomputed hourly from the starting FFMC; ISI from FFMC + wind (ST-X-3); records at every whole hour and at each step [R41] [R42] |
| RPAS perimeter restart | `runner.restart_config` (request weather, else the source's re-based), shared `_prepare_inputs` / `_simulator`; level set restarted from the perimeter's burned cells (signed distance at cell edges, no acceleration) | Owner field tests [R41] [R42]; growth check in §3 (2026-10-12) |
| Fuel-grid vintage check | `runner.fuel_vintage_year` / `fuel_vintage_warning`; UI `utils/fuelVintage.ts` | Grid years §4.3; burn-scar re-typing [R39-fuel] |
| Fire growth, spatial fuel | `spread/cellular.py`: level set advected with the Huygens velocity of each cell's FBP ellipse (second-order ENO upwind) | Richards (1990) [8]; Lautenberger (2013) [9] for the Eulerian level-set framework (eq 1, p.290) only; ELMFIRE's Superbee/RK2 numerics and cosine spread rule (eqs 6-14) not used (`docs/verification.md`) |
| Fire growth, uniform fuel | `spread/huygens.py`: Huygens wavelets, convex hull, 5 min step, 1 m start | Richards (1990) [8] |
| Active / inactive perimeter edges | `spread/cellular.py` (`active_edges`) | FireSim method; harness proxy for an RPAS thermal flight (`docs/validation.md`) |

| Open-data fuel grid v2.1 stand typing (offline) | `scripts/fuelgrid/stands.py`: `class_objects` (one-class connected regions ∩ 200 m blocks), `uplvi_percent_conifer` (CON × STYPEPER over treed components), `StandModel` (LightGBM multiclass, balanced + cross-entropy regressor on object-mean features), `consistent_pc`; `build2.py` out-of-fold by 2 km blocks; pure-stand scoring with the reference-layer scripts | Object-based typing: conifer-mapping report §1.3, §4; labels: uPLVI interpretation manual (CON = percent conifer trees); evaluation design: reference-layer report §2-3 [R26] |

**Deliberate deviations and heuristics**
- FFMC moisture coefficient 147.2 (Van Wagner 1987 eq 2; ST-X-3 eq 46) in the engine (FWI and FBP) and, from 2026-10-10, in the frontend's live ISI (was 147.27723); cffdrs uses 147.27723. **Effect (corrected 2026-10-10; was stated as "ISI changes ≤ 1e-3"):** for a given FFMC, ISI moves by ≤ 0.1 % (relative), so the frontend change is cosmetic; through the daily FFMC recursion the codes differ from cffdrs-147.27723 by up to 0.12 FFMC, 0.26 ISI, 0.25 FWI on the 48-day sequence, and 0.67 ISI / 0.52 FWI on single days near FFMC 97; in hourly FFMC chains up to 1.12 FFMC (480 h cffdrs `hffmc` test, [R15]). With the coefficient swapped the FFMC/ISI/DC differences vanish (tested). The cffdrs fixtures are generated with 147.2 (the FWI fixture also at 147.27723).
- DMC after rain uses Van Wagner (1987) eq 16 as printed (M = 20 + exp(5.6348 − P/43.43)); cffdrs uses the program form 20 + 280/exp(0.023 P). Up to 0.064 DMC difference on rain days (48-day sequence); kept, open item §6.
- Spotting: emission, probability, landing below the maximum, and per-fuel stand sizes (`STAND_DEFAULTS`) are assumptions; terrain correction not implemented; active crown fires use the torching model with several trees (underestimate). The active crown fire model of Albini, Alexander & Cruz (2012) is **not** implemented.
- Ensemble perturbations are symmetric (median 1): the area bias is not corrected, so burn probability is over-confident; curing and FMC sizes are not validated. Per M3 (2026-10-10) the area bias is to be fixed through set-up, and any ROS factor only by fire type and fuel.
- Burning period is a fixed clock window (no overnight runs, ROS × 0 outside it).
- DMC and DC are held fixed within a run.
- Building exposure: blackbody 1200 K, emissivity 1 even for thin surface flames; no embers, no convective heating, no burning buildings or yard fuels.
- Validation harness: CFSDS DOB days are interpolated from satellite passes (hours of timing uncertainty); one ERA5 weather point per fire; 2014b national fuel grid without burn-scar succession; D-1/M-1 → D-2/M-2 between day-of-year 150 and 258 (assumption for Alberta boreal); M-1/M-2 at 50 % conifer; O-1 curing 90 % outside the green season / 60 % inside (the M1 check [R14] also ran 95 % in the spring window and 60 % everywhere).
- WUI zone modifiers in `data/wui_zones.geojson.gz` are unsourced and off by default.

   The 2026-10-17 ember reviews ([R51], [R52]) read about 60 further papers (evidence table and corpus in their data folders). They are not numbered here until code or a merged doc cites them; [R51] §8 and [R52] §9 hold their full references with Zotero keys or corpus ids.

**Internal reports** (owner's research, not peer reviewed; local, not in the repo):
- [R1] *FireSim EOC best practice review* (2026-10-07), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/FireSim EOC best practice review.md`: post-incident reviews (Slave Lake, Fort McMurray, Jasper, Lytton, Marshall, Camp, Lahaina), ranked programme, "avoid" list, name clash with Technosylva's FireSim.
- [R2] *Fuel type grids: Canada and global* (2026-10-07), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Fuel type grids Canada global.md`: Edmonton grid provenance, loader misplacement, OSM water mask, CFS Current-Year national layer, code 13, global options.
- [R5] *Grass diagonal spread fix validation* (2026-10-09), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Grass diagonal spread fix validation 2026-10-09.md`: CFSDS held-out comparison before/after the grid-model diagonal-spread fix (PR #35), set-up, fire-days, F1 tables, commands; raw runs in `$FIRESIM_VALIDATION_DATA/diagfix/`. (R3/R4 are reserved for the structure-ignition reports on another branch.)
- [R3] *Structure ignition in the WUI: a literature review for FireSim* (2026-10-06), `~/dev/wildfire/references/structure-ignition/structure_ignition_review.md`: repo audit of building outputs, structure-ignition literature, the Berkeley/UMD model and its code-only items.
- [R4] *Structure ignition paper check: can FireSim rebuild the Berkeley/UMD WUI model from the literature alone?* (2026-10-09, revised the same day), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Structure ignition paper check.md`: component-by-component check, conflicting published values, recall-only validation (precision 9-77 %), grid-convergence finding, build order. Basis of `docs/structure-spread-spec.md`.
- [R6] *Structure spread: owner decisions and reasoning* (2026-10-09), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Structure spread owner decisions 2026-10-09.md`: decisions D1-D6 made under the owner's delegation (design fire, distance limits, aggregate-only output, footprints, Jasper data, Qin 0.34 m/s), each with its reasoning.
- [R7] *Structure spread: sensitivity to the cutoff, contact distance and footprint filter* (2026-10-09), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Structure spread sensitivity 2026-10-09.md`: decisions D2/D4 follow-up on Edmonton footprints, 3 sites × 2 weather days, Hamada option, aggregate counts only; script `scripts/structure_sensitivity.py`, outputs `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/structure-sensitivity-2026-10-09/`.
- [R8] *Structure spread: front contact measured from the building's own cells* (2026-10-09), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Structure front contact fix 2026-10-09.md`: the building-cell contact rule and why, rerun of the [R7] sensitivity before/after (same settings, seed 20261009), aggregate counts only; outputs `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/structure-front-contact-2026-10-09/`.
- [R9] *Jasper 2024 building data search* (2026-10-09), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Jasper 2024 building data search 2026-10-09.md`: public sources for building-level damage, timing and weather of the Jasper Wildfire Complex (decision D5). The MOJ "Damage Assessment Structures" layer is the observed set (townsite 370 / 16 / 733), with CFS NOR-X-433 and the municipal after-action review for timing. It records the gaps: progression GIS, the FPI per-structure table, station data for 16:00-22:00, and suppression locations. No one was contacted.
- [R10] *Jasper 2024: building-by-building check of FireSim structure spread* (2026-10-10), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Jasper 2024 structure validation 2026-10-10.md`: the pre-registered set-up, data provenance and licence status, results against the baselines, the pre-specified sensitivity, interpretation, limits and the data to request. Script `scripts/jasper_structure_validation.py`; outputs (local only) `~/dev/wildfire/validation-data/jasper2024/results-2026-10-10/`.
- [R11] *Structure spread memory fix* (2026-10-10), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Structure spread memory fix 2026-10-10.md`: the API out-of-memory crash with `structure_spread`, cause (units for every footprint in the grid box), before/after peak RSS, the reachable-box build and its exactness argument, count equivalence on the six sensitivity runs, per-building payload sizes.
- [R12] *FireSim mechanics decisions* (2026-10-10), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Mechanics decisions 2026-10-10.md`: decisions M1-M6 under the owner's delegation (curing default, LiDAR CBH, area bias, ember sources, 20 m grid near buildings, tree inventory), with reasons and order of work.
- [R13] *FireSim mechanics: what the NRES 799 literature says to change* (2026-10-10), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/FireSim mechanics literature review 2026-10-10.md`: read-only review of FireSim's mechanics against the NRES 799 corpus, page-level evidence, ranked recommendations, keep / don't-do lists, corpus gaps.

- [R51] *Ember literature systematic review before v8* (2026-10-17), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Ember literature systematic review before v8 2026-10-17.md`, with its protocol written before any search, `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Ember literature systematic review protocol 2026-10-17.md` (search logs, screening and extraction tables, open-access PDFs and figure in `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/ember-systematic-review-2026-10-17/`): 40 v7 and structure-model choices rated against the literature (2 strong, 12 moderate, 14 weak, 4 guessed, 8 contradicted: match-flame p_e as a per-ember probability, no wind term at the bed, Albini cap on the tail, steady plume, f_w = 0.45 × 20 ft wind, town ROS 0.5 × FBP head ROS, Hamada rates for North American WUI fires, v3 link scale 160 m); the shared Sardoy kernel has never been validated against spot locations; candidate validation datasets; a v8 design scoring where new spot fires appear beyond an observed, re-initialised perimeter (VIIRS spot-jump catalogue, receptivity × distance-decay baseline, ELMFIRE as an outputs-only comparator). Internal, single reviewer, not peer reviewed; no repository commits.
- [R52] *Ember literature ML comparison before v8* (2026-10-17), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Ember literature ML comparison 2026-10-17.md` (data `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/ember-literature-ml-comparison-2026-10-17/`; shared corpus `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/ember-literature-corpus-2026-10-17/`): v7 parameters against 72 hand-curated literature values (inside: n_kg, p_e moisture location, dry threshold, 6 m house-to-house link, 15.6 km long-mode cut; outside: mass yield 100 g/kg, Albini cap ≤ 5.8 km in the structure chain, v3 link scale 160 m); the spot-distance match to Storey is circular; building-loss AUCs in the literature are mostly within-fire (per-fire prediction interval 0.49-0.91; the only leave-one-fire-out test, on FireSim's own five training fires, scores Camp 0.42); ML-extraction precision 32 % (screen, not source); survey of ML validation designs. **§6 corrects the systematic review [R51]:** (1) E3's "P = 0 without wind" source (Sun et al. 2018) is about cigarette butts; Yang et al. 2021 p.9 supports E3 with real firebrands; (2) the Albini cap applies only to the structure chain; v7 wildland spots are cut at 15.6 km, so T5 stands for the structure chain only; (3) E2: keep the p_e curve's moisture location, fix its magnitude and add wind; (4) G2: the same source also gives 59 /kg, and Wickramasinghe 2022 gives about 58-75 /kg; (5) G4 "weak" supported (field mean 0.15 g, mode 10-20 mg); (6) V3: AUC 0.70-0.80 is realistic only as a multi-fire average; (7) Southwell 2026 upgraded from abstract to full text; (8) Hoecker 2026's per-fire 0.80 is right, the abstract's 0.83 is burn probability; (9) **§5.2 power:** Storey's 2.3 spots per fire counts spots beyond **500 m** in fires with ≥ 3 spots; beyond 1 km it is about 1.7 per fire before VIIRS detection losses, so about 180 jumps from 80 fires is optimistic; (10) Syphard 2012's AUCs are Maxent hazard maps, not house-loss models.
- [R53] *Dixie early-arrival diagnostic* (2026-10-18), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Dixie early-arrival diagnostic 2026-10-18.md` (README, `_about.json`, scripts in `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/dixie-early-arrival-diagnostic-2026-10-18/`): post-hoc, not pre-registered; PREREG_LR7 and its scores unchanged (PR #87). Why every model reaches 17 of Dixie's 60 scored blocks 16-28 h early: four places just outside the start perimeter; the observed times are real (no VIIRS night detections, GOFER agrees, a public account times Lake Almanor West at about 28 h); documented line-holding and structure defence on perimeter segments that did not move; systematic for every perimeter start (static share of the start perimeter 59-74 % observed vs 5-10 % modelled); three coding issues (3 blocks outside the final perimeter, 18 left-censored blocks, 8 single-detection times) that do not cause the bias.

### 4.3 Data sources

| Source | Use | Licence |
|---|---|---|
| Edmonton FBP fuel grid `data/Edmonton_FBP_FuelLayer_20251105_10m.tif` | Spatial fuel (codes 2 C-2, 12 D-2, 14 M-2, 31 O-1a, 32 O-1b, 99 non-fuel; `canopy_lidar` scheme) | **Open Government Licence – City of Edmonton v1.0** (licence check 2026-10-12, [R38] §1; was recorded as a City-internal product until then). Every input is City of Edmonton open data under the portal's terms of use: *2025 Lidar – Full Feature* ([data.edmonton.ca `sz3i-y5ze`](https://data.edmonton.ca/dataset/sz3i-y5ze), flown 29-30 June 2025, leaf-on, 25 pts/m², EPSG:3776; published 2026-06-12), *Urban Primary Land Vegetation Inventory – 2025* (`gm9t-5epd`), *Vegetation Areas – Naturalized* (`ct88-r8tk`), and the conifer/deciduous crown classifier's labels, the City *Trees* inventory (`eecg-fc54`, 474,289 trees in the March 2026 snapshot used). Attribution: *"Contains information licensed under the Open Government Licence – Edmonton."* (shown in the app's About). Caveat: the build read a vegetation-classified LAZ copy of the 2025 acquisition (3,233 tiles), while the portal item is described as GeoTIFF; same acquisition, but the classified point cloud is **not** the published deliverable: the portal's 2025 items are 0.2 m GeoTIFF rasters only (checked 2026-10-12, [R40] §4.3); the grid is kept with a corrected notice and written City confirmation is recommended (§6). Lineage [R2], corrected 2026-10-12: **20 m** cells, EPSG:3776, built 2026-03-20 by `scripts/build_fuel_raster.py` in the `edmonton-burnp3` repo (`~/dev/wildfire/edmonton-burnp3`, commit `223dd29`; not the Urban-Forestry LiDAR repo) from tree crowns delineated in the 2025 LiDAR, a conifer/deciduous Random Forest on LiDAR crown features trained on the Trees inventory, LiDAR canopy cover, and uPLVI / Naturalized Areas polygons; committed to FireSim 2026-04-05 (`c1f3804`). No 2019 LiDAR and no internal City tree table was found in that chain. Filename (date, "10m") is wrong. Never accuracy-assessed |
| Alberta Wildfire – FBP Fuel Type – Raster (`WildfireRasters.GDB.WF_FBP_FUEL_TYPE`, dataset date 2025-07-16; 100 m, EPSG:3400, int16, 7,856 × 15,002), as the COG `ab_wildfire_fbp_fueltype_2025_100m_3400_cog.tif` (31.8 MB, SHA-256 `e9555f40…`, release `data-ab-fbp-2025`, not committed) | Default fuel where the City grid has no data and outside it (2026-10-11) | © Government of Alberta. **Open Government Licence – Alberta** as stated on the map-service item `4fa5b04edebb4f6e883025e0add3b57e`; the image-service item `689aac9591cb4f31a9178be663f22ee2` carries only the GoA disclaimer (as is, no warranty). Attribution shown: "Contains information licensed under the Open Government Licence – Alberta." Lineage "Unknown due to different data sources"; partly AVI-derived. Downloaded 2026-10-10, class counts equal the attribute table |
| `data/edmonton_dem.tif` | Slope and aspect | 30 m, EPSG:26912, TIFF date 2011; recorded as Open Government Canada, exact product not recorded (likely NRCan CDEM; **unverified**) |
| `data/edmonton_buildings.geojson.gz` | Building mask, exposure, building index | Microsoft Canadian Building Footprints, ODbL (file metadata: 346,238 footprints, generated 2025-11-16; corrected 2026-10-08, `45e68d2`; was listed as City Open Data). Its `type`/`height`/`material`/`roof_type` attributes have no documented source and are not used |
| `ab_buildings_ms2019.fgb` (release asset `data-ab-buildings-ms2019`, 372,228,480 bytes, SHA-256 `14f0d6760665d332d74917798071bef893dd372b2b6b9f217a82c907da4405f8`; built by `scripts/build_ab_buildings.py` from `Alberta.zip` SHA-256 `4fd2b43d…`) | Buildings for all of Alberta, read by window: mask, exposure, inside-perimeter counts, structure spread [R25] | Microsoft Canadian Building Footprints (2019), © Microsoft, ODbL 1.0; the derived file stays ODbL, attribution in `buildings_source` |

### 4.4 Validation results (summary, updated 2026-10-17)

Every validation and pre-registered test to date, aggregate only (plain-language scorecard: `docs/OVERVIEW.md` §3). CIs are 95 % (fire bootstrap for CFSDS; 250 m / block bootstrap for building tests). "Pass" = the stated decision rule or goal met; "fail" = not met; "void" = the test could not be run as registered; "n/a" = model behaviour or agreement, not a validation. The detailed entries below the table are the earlier §4.4 text, kept unchanged.

| Date | Test | Data / fire | Result (95 % CI) | Pass / fail / void | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-05/07 | FBP + FWI equations vs `cffdrs` | 12,960 FBP cases; FWI 48-day sequence + 21 edge cases | rel. diff ≤ 3e-10 (FBP); FFMC/ISI/DC ≤ 1e-13 | pass (verification) | `docs/verification.md` §1 |
| 2026-10-07 | Spread vs FBP ellipse and WISE | uniform fuel | area 0.91-1.07 × ellipse; WISE area ratio 0.955-1.09 | pass (verification) | `docs/verification.md` §2-3 |
| 2026-10-07 | Baseline one-day growth | CFSDS, 143 fire-days, 32 AB fires | F1 0.15 (perimeter start) / 0.24 (Bennett start); WISE defaults 0.26; over-predicted on 93 % of days | n/a (baseline) | `docs/validation.md` |
| 2026-10-07 | Held-out set-up (active edges + spin-up + 10-20 h) | 16 test fires, 79 fire-days | F1 0.118 → 0.208 (+0.091, +0.056 to +0.125); area bias +0.72 → +0.27 | pass (skill gain); absolute skill low | `docs/validation.md` |

**Headline (2026-10-15, [R45]):** held-out one-day F1 of the operational set-up is **0.246** on the pre-fire back-cast grid (the fuel FireSim uses for dated historical Alberta runs) and 0.212 on CFS 2014b (former headline, kept as comparator). Growth is still over-predicted on most days (73 % held-out, chosen set-up; 94 % whole perimeter). **Ensemble (2026-10-17, [R49]):** on the back-cast grid, P10-P90 coverage 49 % (2014b 48 %, no detectable difference), CRPS of log10 area 0.407 (2014b 0.465; −0.057, −0.113 to −0.007, better), spread/skill 1.34, P10 holds ≥ 90 % of growth on 28 % of days: still too narrow and over-confident. Earlier rows keep the grid they were measured on. **By start type (2026-10-18, [R58], descriptive):** of the 79 held-out fire-days, 5 are point starts (F1 0.402), 4 fresh perimeters (0.306) and 70 mature perimeters (0.231); over-spread grows with start age (median growth ratio 0.97 / 1.32 / 2.22, all 143 fire-days), while the static share of mature start perimeters is close to observed (97 % vs 99 %) under the active-edge set-up.

**Not yet measured:** multi-day growth on observed fires; live (undated) runs on the 2025 grid against observed fires; RPAS-corrected mid-day restarts and real thermal-flight active edges; ensemble skill on the largest runs; the curing default against Edmonton grass-fire records; field accuracy of any fuel grid; building exposure against observed losses; code 105 on 2026+ fires.

#### Detail (earlier §4.4 entries, 2026-10-07 to 2026-10-11)


- **20 m WUI window (2026-10-10; M5; model behaviour, not a validation)** [R18]: CFSDS held-out validation not rerun (its 250 m national grid is refined to 90 m, so nearest neighbour stays and inputs are byte-identical; no buildings, so the window never applies); Jasper not rerun (structure-only, no fuel grid). Uniform fuel, 1 h: area vs FBP ellipse 50 m / 20 m: C-2 −4.4 / −4.3 % (W wind), −6.3 / −3.8 % (SW); O-1a −9.5 / −6.5 % (W), −10.1 / −7.8 % (SSW), −7.8 / −5.8 % (SW). Six Edmonton runs, 6 h, area 50 m nearest / 50 m mode / 20 m: A 2.0 / 0.0 / 10.2 and 11.0 / 7.0 / 22.3 ha; B 21.0 / 15.5 / 21.1 and 59.3 / 61.8 / 82.1; C 32.3 / 30.3 / 59.1 and 94.8 / 115.5 / 149.5 (moderate / extreme). Without the building mask the 20 m areas change ≤ 3 %, so the increase is fuel detail. Front-contact gap median 22-42 → 9-15 m, max 64-78 → 24-30 m; involved at 6 h −52 % to +33 % vs [R16]; contact 20 m now +9 to +68 % (meaningful again at 20 m); 0 ember ignitions in all 42 runs.

- **Data:** 143 fire-days, 32 Alberta fires 2014-2024 (CFSDS), one burn day from the observed perimeter, Bennett et al. (2026) protocol, grid model, deterministic, no suppression.
- **Baseline (first round):** one-day F1 at the default 06-23 h window **0.15** (whole perimeter start, operational) and **0.24** with Bennett's ignition (W.I.S.E. defaults 0.26 nationally; tuned W.I.S.E. 0.54). Growth over-predicted on 93 % of days (perimeter start), normalised area difference +0.67; head bearing error median ~50°; forward spread within ±35 % on ~20 % of days. On nine shared fire-days with identical inputs: W.I.S.E. 0.19 vs FireSim 0.24.
- **Held-out (second round):** fires split by seeded hash (16 calibration / 16 test fires, 79 test fire-days), settings chosen on calibration only. Active edges (previous 2 days) + FFMC spin-up + burning period 10-20 h: test F1 **0.118 → 0.208** (ΔF1 +0.091, 95 % CI +0.056 to +0.125, bootstrap by fire); area difference +0.72 → +0.27; spread within ±35 % on 24 % of days. Oracle start 0.245. Largest wind-driven runs (Horse River 4-5 May 2016) under-predicted further.
- **Grid diagonal-spread fix (2026-10-09, PR #35):** re-run of the chosen set-up and the Bennett start, all 143 fire-days, before (`a8f8f35`) and after (`4bcfb1d`); "before" reproduced the numbers above exactly. Held-out 17 h F1: active edges + spin-up + 10-20 h **0.208 → 0.212** (ΔF1 +0.004, 95 % CI −0.001 to +0.009); Bennett start + spin-up 0.245 → 0.250 (+0.005, +0.000 to +0.011). Earlier windows dip slightly (8 h −0.003 / −0.008; best hour −0.004 / −0.007, CIs exclude zero but tiny). Skill effectively unchanged; the fix is a correctness fix (grid-orientation independence), not a skill change [R5].
- **Ensemble (third round, held-out; measured before the grid fix):** 20 members on the chosen set-up, 65 test fire-days (14 largest excluded by run time only). Calibrated sizes vs former placeholders: observed area inside members' P10-P90 **20 % → 49 %** (ideal ~80 %); CRPS of log10 area 0.546 → **0.471** (single run 0.616); Brier skill vs single run +0.18 → +0.25; spread/skill 4.7 → 1.6 (still too narrow); cells at ~95 % burn probability burned 33 % of the time; P50 F1 0.201 vs single run 0.195; **P10 footprint held ≥ 90 % of observed growth on 29 % of days**, so P10 is not a worst case.
- **Structure-spread sensitivity (2026-10-09; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R7]: Hamada option, Edmonton footprints (334,192 units), 3 WUI sites (mature ravine edge with garages; lower-density ravine edge; newer suburb beside grass) × 2 days (FWI 15 and 72), 6 h, deterministic FBP run shared by all variants. Involved buildings at 6 h vs the defaults (30 m cutoff, 10 m contact, all footprints), over the 5 runs with a non-zero default: cutoff 20 m **−48 % to 0 %**, 45 m **+5 % to +220 %** (between 30 and 45 m the unit graph joins blocks across streets: largest component 2.5 % → 7.5 % of units); contact 5 m **−71 % to 0 %**, 20 m **+1 % to +557 %** (and 0 → 148 on one moderate run); dropping footprints < 40 m² **−8 % to 0 %**. The contact result is grid-dependent: on the 50 m engine grid with the all-touched building mask the nearest burnable cell lies 0-50 m from a footprint depending on where it sits in its cell. Defaults unchanged; a change to how contact is measured is recommended (§6). City-wide graph at 30 m: median nearest separation 2.56 m (2.58 m without < 40 m²), median degree 8, 1.6 % with no neighbour. Run time: whole city units 3-6 s, spread < 0.3 s per variant; the script's default reproduces the engine's own counts exactly and repeats identically.
- **Structure front contact from the building cells (2026-10-09; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R8]: same sites, days, seed and FBP runs as [R7] (a rerun of the unchanged script reproduced [R7] exactly). With contact measured from each building's grid cells (`11c1230`): contact 5 / 20 m **0 %** in all six runs (the contact distance is below the 50 m grid's resolution); involved buildings at 6 h vs the first rule **+38 % to +751 %** (site A moderate 0 → 199; front contacts 2-6×; front-contacted footprints a median 22-42 m, at most ~70 m, from the nearest burned cell edge); cutoff 20 m **−58 % to −27 %**, 45 m **+4 % to +170 %**; dropping footprints < 40 m² **−12 % to 0 %**. The cutoff is now the main structural sensitivity; defaults unchanged. The script's default again matches the engine in all runs; the contact rule costs ~2 s per variant over the whole city.
- **Jasper 2024 structure check (2026-10-10; first test against observed Canadian losses)** [R10]: end-state, structure-only, ember entry imposed, pre-registered (§3).
  - **Primary run:** 15 seeds at 18:00, cutoff 30 m, wind 15 km/h SW, 18:00-24:00. 396 involved against 370 destroyed (358 official). TP 250 / FP 146 / FN 120 / TN 603; precision **63.1 %**, recall **67.6 %**, F1 65.3 %, **κ 0.472**.
  - **Baselines (κ):**

    | Baseline | κ |
    |---|---|
    | Count-matched distance band from the seeds | **0.516** |
    | Distance band with N = observed | 0.499 |
    | FPI < 5 m, one step | 0.025 |
    | FPI ≤ 5 m percolation | 0.095 |
    | Random at the observed rate | ≈ 0 |
    | Seeds only (no spread) | 0.011 |

  - **κ differences (250 m block bootstrap):** FireSim − band −0.044 (95 % −0.157 to +0.045); FireSim − seeds only +0.461 (+0.238 to +0.649).
  - **Pre-registered rule not met:** given the ember-entry locations, Hamada does no better than distance from them.
  - **Sensitivity (12 pre-specified runs):**
    - κ 0.19-0.51, precision 55-84 %, recall 22-92 %, involved count 124-593;
    - the cutoff dominates: 20 m gives 124 (κ 0.19), 45 m gives 593 (κ 0.51);
    - wind ±25 % and wind from SSW change little, because below 10 m/s Hamada is nearly isotropic (Hazus blend);
    - FireSim − band runs from −0.044 to +0.068, and every interval includes zero.
  - **Seeds and outcome:** scoring without the seeds, or with Destroyed + Visible Damage, changes κ by ≤ 0.03.
  - **Errors** sit at 250-500 m from the seeds:
    - FN: separate ember-ignited groups (not modelled);
    - FP: at the north-east front towards the commercial core, where the after-action review records demolitions at 21:30 (not recorded, so not testable).
  - **Graph:** at 5 / 20 / 30 m the largest component holds 1.6 / 8.6 / 49.7 % of units.
  - **Context only:** precision is in the range of FSJ104651 (59 / 9 / 77 %).
- **Grass curing default (2026-10-10; M1)** [R14]: chosen set-up, all 143 fire-days, only O-1 curing changed.
  - **Held-out 17 h F1:** 60 % everywhere (old default) **0.2126** → M1 (95 % in day of year 60-149; 60 % summer and 90 % fall as stand-ins for the user's entry) **0.2120**; ΔF1 −0.0007 (95 % CI −0.0017 to −0.0000). Area difference +0.246 → +0.248. Calibration 0.2251 → 0.2285. The former harness rule (90 / 60) gives 0.2120 and reproduces the recorded number.
  - **Spring-window fire-days** (the only ones M1 changes, with 2 fall days): 5 held-out from 3 fires −0.009 [−0.016, +0.000]; 9 calibration (6 Horse River) +0.024 [−0.010, +0.135], mostly one day where 60 % stopped the fire.
  - **Grass involvement:** O-1 is 3.4 % of all observed growth; present in the growth of 76 of 143 fire-days, ≥ 5 % of the growth on 23. Boreal fires: the harness cannot test the 95 % value.
  - **Sensitivity:** a silent 90 % in summer lowers held-out F1 (−0.008 [−0.019, −0.000]) and raises area over-prediction (+0.25 → +0.30), which supports no summer default.
- **Jasper 2024 ember check (2026-10-10; pre-registered, `ccce4d6`)** [R16]: Hamada + embers on the same set-up as [R10] (structure-only; no wildland ember source).

Each Hamada + embers run equals its Hamada-only reference: primary κ **0.472** (precision 63.1 %, recall 67.6 %), distance band 0.516, difference −0.044 (−0.157 to +0.045). Decision rule not met.
  - Model-only diagnostic: at 15 km/h ψ* = 0.178 g/cm² (~1.4 million embers on a median 154 m² footprint) against 0.46 million emitted by such a building over its whole 150 kW/m² fire (2.4 million at 400); the largest ember pool on any building not otherwise involved reached 4.5 % (150) / 14 % (400) of its threshold. Embers alone ignite a few buildings only at 400 kW/m² and ≥ 40 km/h.
- **Edmonton ember rerun (2026-10-10; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R16]: `scripts/structure_sensitivity.py --embers`, same 3 sites x 2 days, seed and FBP runs as [R7]/[R8] (Hamada counts reproduced: 199 / 315 / 83 / 454 / 308 / 653 at 6 h). Seven ember variants (default 150 kW/m² with wildland embers; GR' 5.68; 400 kW/m²; cutoff 20 / 45 m; structures-only at 150 and 400): **0 ember-ignited buildings in all 42 runs**, involved counts identical to the matching Hamada variants, including the extreme days (max HFI 78-82 MW/m, wildland Sardoy embers on). The engine's reachable build matched the whole-city build in all six default ember runs (2,606-5,576 units built instead of 334,192).

VB − H30 +0.009 (+0.004 to +0.014); VB − (a) +0.004 (+0.000 to +0.009); VB − (e1) +0.001 (−0.002 to +0.005). **Decision rule not met** (interval vs e1 includes zero), and the absolute skill is negligible. Roof scenarios and embers: no ember ignition, counts unchanged.   - **Held-out 17 h F1 (79 days):** 2014b **0.212**; open-data masked (primary) **0.192**, ΔF1 −0.020 [−0.066, +0.023], no detectable difference (all 143 days 0.182, −0.037 [−0.069, −0.005]); CFS 2026 masked 0.170 (−0.042 [−0.087, −0.006]); open vs CFS 2026 masked +0.021 [+0.002, +0.044] (open better). Within ±35 %: 22 / 22 / 16 %; median abs bearing error 38° / 51° / 62°.
  - **Mask artefact:** nearest-cell filling puts 21-22 % of the observed growth on non-fuel (2014b 5 %). Pre-fire open grid (fires 2021-2024, 56 days): 0.236 vs 2014b 0.202 (+0.034 [−0.008, +0.082], no detectable difference) and vs its masked self +0.082 [+0.027, +0.152].
  - **Leakage runs:** unmasked open 0.216 (scar becomes O-1b); unmasked CFS 2026 0.107 (scar mapped as post-fire / non-fuel).
  - **Fuel typing:** the open grid is 64 % O-1b over the fire areas (rule 7; 25 % treed stands); of 2014b C-2 growth 59 % and of C-1 growth 80 % is O-1b in the pre-fire grid. 22.6 % of the observed growth lies in classes it cannot represent (C-1 18.7 %, wetland 3.8 %, C-3 0.1 %); urban rules ≈ 0.5 % of the area. Equal F1 despite this shows one-day F1 depends weakly on fuel type here.
  - **Disclosed extra (not pre-registered):** Alberta Wildfire 2025 grid, masked: held-out 0.160 (−0.052 [−0.104, −0.006]); a lower bound, since no fire post-dates it. Open-data v2 (#54) not run.
- **Not yet measured:** RPAS-corrected mid-day restarts, real thermal-flight active edges, ensemble skill on the largest runs; the curing default against Edmonton grass-fire records; field accuracy of any fuel grid.

- **Demonstration, not validation: SWF-065 two-stage case in one run (2026-10-12)** [R41] [R42]. Inputs: ignition 55.2387 N, 114.6896 W; start 2011-05-14 17:50 MDT; weather schedule 47 km/h from 135° until 20:00, then 20 km/h; 20 °C, RH 15 %; FFMC 90, DMC 15, DC 193; curing 90 %; 50 % conifer; burning period 10:00-23:00; Alberta fuel grid (2025, post-fire: the run carries the fuel-vintage warning) with buildings and terrain; 6 h, 30 min frames; local API on the branch (`demo.py`). Spotting off / on (1.0):
  - **Schedule 47 → 20 km/h:** 529.5 / 575.3 ha at 23:50; head ROS up to 120.6 / 120.5 m/min before 20:00, then 49-63 m/min (spotting off); Slave Lake (census subdivision) within 500 m by **21:02 / 20:59**; head stalled at Poplar Estates 18:50-19:50 (0.9 m/min).
  - **Constant 47 km/h (same run without the schedule):** 496.5 / 539.0 ha (the owner's live Poplar run: 539.0 ha); head 110-125 m/min; Slave Lake within 500 m by 21:04 / 21:00 (owner: 21:00).
  - **Why the 20 km/h phase is not the owner's 30 m/min:** with a schedule FFMC is recomputed hourly from 90 and dries at 20 °C / RH 15 % (91.8 at 20:00, 93.5 at 23:50), so ISI at 20 km/h is 16-19 instead of 11.7 and O-1b runs at about 50-60 m/min; the owner's Stage B kept FFMC 90. The town label is reached at about the same time with or without the wind drop because the fire is already near the census subdivision's south-east boundary when the wind drops (the subdivision extends well south-east of the built-up town, [R42] §6). Area stays under the documented ~1,200 ha; the town label still fires on May 14 (the real fire reached town on May 15). The fuel grid is the 2025 Alberta grid, mostly O-1b regrowth in the 2011 scar (item C, later): not a test of the model.

## 5. Testing

| Suite | Command | What it checks |
|---|---|---|
| Engine (pytest) | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest engine/tests -q` | FBP vs cffdrs fixture, FWI + hourly FFMC, FBP ellipse agreement, WISE reference, level set, deployment data (head/flank/back), active edges, diurnal, ensemble, Albini worked examples, Cohen exposure, loaders/reprojection, national code schemes, validation metrics |
| API (pytest) | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest api/tests -q` | Endpoints, WebSocket frames, grid runs, FWI, weather, skill options |
| Frontend types | `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` | TypeScript (the root `tsconfig.json` has `files: []` + references, so plain `npx tsc --noEmit` checks nothing; CI's `npm run build` runs `tsc -b`) |
| Frontend unit (Vitest) | `cd frontend && npm test` | Classes, contrast, time zone, evac arrival, assets, ICS outputs, skill options, curing default |
| Frontend E2E (Playwright + axe) | `cd frontend && npx playwright test` | Production build via `vite preview`; API mocked by `tests/e2e/mockApi.ts` replaying `tests/fixtures/terwillegar_grass_4h.json` (house-to-house spread: `structure_spread_4h.json`, `record_fixture.py --structure`); external requests blocked; axe baseline, 12 px / 24 px layout rules, idle-redraw performance budget |
| Pyra parity | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest api/tests/test_pyra_source.py`; with `PYRA_DIR=<Pyra checkout>` and node, Pyra's engine is re-run live | FireSim's "pyra" tier vs Pyra's own engine (`initFWI` in Node via Pyra's test harness, `scripts/pyra/run_pyra_parity.mjs`) on recorded inputs (`api/tests/fixtures/pyra_parity.json`, built by `scripts/pyra/build_parity_fixture.py`); daily-step equations; caching, fallbacks, labels |
| Validation (not CI) | `scripts/validate.py prepare / run / report / compare` | Skill on observed fires (§4.4) |

**Oracle / reference provenance**
- **cffdrs daily FWI fixture** `engine/tests/fwi/data/cffdrs_fwi_reference.json`, generated by `generate_cffdrs_fwi_reference.py` from `cffdrs_py` (`CFFDRS_PY_PATH`; `cffdrs.fwi`, `lat_adjust=False`): the cffdrs `tests/data/fwi_test_data.csv` 48-day sequence (the R package's `test_fwi`, lat 40, April-June 1985, start 85/6/15) with its one-decimal values, and 21 edge cases, each at 147.2 and 147.27723. Tolerances: FFMC/ISI/DC 1e-9; DMC 0.07, BUI 0.07, FWI 0.025 (DMC rain-phase form, §4.1), exact without DMC rain.
- **cffdrs fixture** `engine/tests/fbp/data/` generated by `engine/tests/fbp/data/generate_cffdrs_reference.py` from a `cffdrs_py` checkout (`CFFDRS_PY_PATH`), FFMC coefficient 147.2; 18 fuels × 6 weather/slope cases in CI, 12,960 cases in a development run; hourly FFMC 5,760 cases (max diff 3e-11).
- **WISE reference** `engine/tests/spread/data/wise_reference.json`: WISE 1.0.6-beta.6 (official Ubuntu build, headless) on uniform C-2 / O-1a, wind 0/20/30 km/h, flat and sloped planes (built at 30.5/40.5 % because WISE truncates slope to integer percent).
- **Cohen worked values:** SIAM 50 m × 20 m flame at 1200 K → 79 / 45 / 27 kW/m² at 10 / 20 / 30 m and ~60 s at 31 kW/m² (Cohen 2004); FireSim 80.6 / 45.9 / 27.8 and 59 s.
- **Albini/Chase worked examples:** Chase 1981 torching 0.34 mi; Albini 1983 surface fire 0.45 km.
- **CFSDS validation:** Barber et al. (2024) DOB rasters; metrics unit-tested on shapes with known answers; W.I.S.E. comparison numbers from Bennett et al. (2026) Table 1 plus nine local W.I.S.E. runs.
- **Frontend fixture:** recorded from the real engine by `npm run fixture:record` (`frontend/tests/fixtures/record_fixture.py`).

**Previous results (PR #56), CI race fix (2026-10-11, branch `fix/rerun-and-multiday`):** CI (Python 3.12) failed `test_multiday_incremental_frames_rebuild_cumulative` with 22 of 25 frames under status "completed". Cause, a real API race (not the test): `GET /simulations/{id}` read the frames and then the status, so a run finishing while the frames were serialised was reported "completed" with its last frames missing (a polling client then stops with an incomplete run); the WebSocket replay had the same order. Fix: the status is read before the frames in GET and in the WebSocket replay (frames are always added before the terminal status), and a run that fails after the replay snapshot now gets its `simulation.error`. Regression test `test_get_never_reports_completed_with_frames_missing` (the run finishes exactly between the two reads). API pytest then **201 passed, 1 skipped**.

## 6. Open items

- [ ] **Rescore follow-ups (2026-10-15, [R45]):** (a) a live run's fuel (the 2025 grid without a back-cast) cannot be scored fairly on past fires, so quote 0.246 only for historical replays; (b) the deciduous-day gain on the back-cast comes with area bias +0.08 → +0.47 (18 days, 8 fires): check the back-cast's conifer/mixedwood typing of 2014b's aspen stands against AVI; (c) owner review of the suppression classes, then decide whether to pre-register a free-burning headline (excluding suppression-dominated / burnout days; exploratory value 0.256 held-out); (d) `eoc-bridge.spec.ts:36` Playwright failure (`page.reload: net::ERR_ABORTED`) reproduces locally (3 of 3 runs) with frontend code unchanged from `master` `f4cdd17` but passes in CI: investigate the local environment; (e) ~~finish E1, the ensemble rescore~~ done 2026-10-17 ([R49]: CRPS better, coverage no detectable difference); (f) new, from [R49]: the ensemble sizes (A15) were calibrated on 2014b; on the back-cast the spread/skill fell to 1.34 while coverage stayed 49 %, so re-check the inflation factor on the back-cast calibration fires before quoting a re-calibrated ensemble (not pre-registered yet).
- [ ] **Pre-fire back-cast follow-ups (2026-10-13, [R44]):** (a) build SCANFI epochs 1985-2005 (`python -m scripts.fuelgrid.backcast mirror|epoch 2005 …`, ~1.5 GB of SCANFI each) so scenarios 1986-2010 are back-cast, then re-package the release; (b) map test of the typing against AVI where the photo predates the burn ([R39-fuel] §9.1), and an ecozone layer for the taiga C-1 rules; (c) young-burn rule for burns before the scenario and harvest since it (CanLaD / AVI cut blocks); (d) Slave Lake: the head follows a 100 m O-1 band beside Highway 2 outside the NBAC burn — check how much highway / right-of-way O-1 is narrower than a cell, and measure town arrival to the built-up area as well as the CSD; (e) the frontend shows the back-cast only through the vintage warning text; a fuel-legend label "as of <date>" is not built.
- [ ] **Restart and schedule follow-ups (2026-10-12, [R41] [R42]):** (a) a restart from a *modelled* perimeter could carry the parent run's arrival field instead of rebuilding the front at cell edges (residual +1-2 % uniform, +7-10 % heterogeneous fuel, more where the head is stalled at a gap); (b) a restart restarts FFMC from Setup's daily code, not the hourly FFMC the parent run had reached; (c) ensemble on restarts (not offered); (d) the ignition cell of a grid run can show 0 ha at T+0 when its level-set value starts at the ellipse edge (seen with a weather schedule; cosmetic, first frame only); (e) live check of the WebSocket fix after deploy (restarts and runs end on their last frame); (f) remaining owner bugs not in this PR: 5 (uniform fuel hides other physics), 7 (fuel layer outside Edmonton), 8 (toggle label), 12 (geocoder), 13 (server progress / frozen timer); (g) fuel vintage: burned-since fraction from NBAC and the back-cast "fuel grid as of X" option ([R39-fuel], item C).
- [ ] **Hamlets and localities (2026-10-12, [R36]):** (a) the 250 m disc for CGNDB points is an assumed footprint (lower quartile of hamlet size), not measured per place; a reached locality is marked by the crossing dot, its disc is never drawn, and an evacuation status set on one shows as a map label only (no outline); (b) localities are not clickable on the map (no polygon); Planning sets their status from the card or the picker, which now lists ~2,200 names; (c) CGNDB coordinates are to the second (~30 m) and some localities are historical crossroads with few or no residents; (d) StatCan designated places are 2021 delineations; the Alberta hamlet layer is revised irregularly (last 2025-02-04) — rebuild with `scripts/build_ab_places.py` when it changes; (e) the crossing rule uses perimeter-frame granularity for Huygens runs (a place reached anywhere in the first frame that touches the county counts as the crossing).
- [ ] **Multi-day / EOC bridge / storage follow-ups (QA 2026-10-11) [R28]:** ~~server-side run archive~~ (built, [R30]; durable deploy pending, see below) then server incidents with 209/IAP snapshots and an append-only audit log (~1-1.5 weeks), team sharing after authentication; Day N+1 should continue from Day N's perimeter (today only a ghost); multi-day ignores `buildings_path` (no exposure); ICS forms hard-code "Jurisdiction: Edmonton"; annotations do not carry to the next period (owner decision); measure API RSS with 40 stored grid runs.

Code: perimeter-override and the WebSocket from an archived source run (needed for "Continue from Day N"); index rebuild from `meta.json` when the index is lost; measure archived sizes for real Edmonton grid runs; decide retention for EOC records under the City records schedule; access control before anything beyond run IDs is exposed. *Phase 2 (2026-10-12):* the list, rename and delete are behind the shared passphrase; ~~`GET /simulations/{id}` keyed only by an 8-hex-character ID~~ (closed 2026-10-13, branch `fix/long-run-ids`: new IDs 128 bits, old 8-hex IDs valid and slowed by a per-IP backoff; the backoff counts are in memory and reset on restart); renamed labels are not written to `meta.json` (an index rebuilt from the store keeps run-time labels only); the failed-attempt counts are in memory and reset on restart.

- [ ] **Open-data fuel grid in the boreal (2026-10-11, [R27]):** v1 does not beat the national 2014b grid on observed fires and maps most boreal conifer as O-1b (stand rule needs ≥ 5 m CHM canopy at ≥ 40 % cover; rule 7 sends WorldCover tree cover to O-1b); no C-1, C-3/C-4 or wetland classes; Sentinel-2 spring/autumn windows are snow-covered at 56-60° N. Before any boreal use: a forest rule independent of CHM height, the missing classes, boreal labels (AVI). The pre-registered nearest-cell scar fill penalises every modern grid; a fairer test needs pre-fire versions of each grid (or fires after a grid's date: the Alberta 2025 grid needs 2025-2026 CFSDS fires). One-day F1 is a weak test of fuel typing; check fuel maps at stand level.
- [ ] **Structure step 3 follow-ups** [R23]: (1) live-server vegetation outside Edmonton: compute ring metrics / canopy raster per run area (a CHM tile is ~0.5 GB; offline script exists); (2) the Eaton structure-only set-up from the documented areas misses the losses entirely (seeds ±1 km from maps; overnight wildland and ember entry not modelled): a coupled FBP + structure run or timed perimeters are needed before any structure rule can be judged there; (3) the windward-band sensitivity (VB +0.19 κ over H30 at Eaton) deserves a pre-registered test against the distance and distance + vegetation baselines; (4) reachable-box vs whole-city mismatch of 1-9 units on two Edmonton runs (different local frames suspected, not verified); (5) roof factor k = 0.375 is a lab bound read as a threshold [H]; a measured shake-roof ψ* or the MOJ pre-fire roof table would replace the scenario.

The restored Setup is per browser (localStorage), not per server incident.
- [ ] **Multi-day runs (2026-10-11):** still no ensemble, no structure spread and no 20 m WUI window (each day is a 50 m deterministic run); FWI carry-over uses one noon record per day (no hourly weather, no spin-up). Run time on the Fly machine not measured (locally a 3-day Edmonton grid run takes 21 s with a 10-20 burning period, 76 s without; 2-day uniform C-2 5.7 s) — measure it after deploy. The stale re-run view reported on 2026-10-11 did not reproduce against the mocked API; if it recurs, capture the browser's network log (the multi-day POST, its response) and the API log. An abandoned ensemble is stopped only when the client sends DELETE; a closed tab still leaves it running (a server-side rule, e.g. stop when nobody has polled for N minutes, is an owner decision). A 30-member 4 h Edmonton ensemble took 515 s on the workstation at the Terwillegar ignition, far above the "about 1 s per member" in `docs/api-reference.md` (a 5-member run there: 1.5 s per member); check the per-member cost distribution.

- [ ] **Ensemble still under-dispersed and over-confident** (held-out spread/skill 1.6; ~95 % burn probability → 33 % burned). **Fix area bias through set-up (active edges, burning period, spin-up); any ROS correction only by fire type and fuel** (M3, 2026-10-10 [R12]; reworded from "remove the shared over-prediction bias"). Evidence [R13] rec 3: FBP under-predicts spread in 75 % of 49 datasets (Cruz & Alexander 2013, p.16), on the ICFME crown fires (Stocks et al. 2004, p.1557) and on Alberta 2023 C-2 runs (Beverly & Schroeder 2025, p.8: predicted 18.7 m/min); FireSim's own Horse River head runs reach 0.1-0.4 of observed; the area over-prediction comes from default burn conditions and the whole perimeter being active (Bennett et al. 2026, p.9). A ROS factor, if any, is estimated per FBP fire type (surface / crown) and fuel group on calibration fires, reported per stratum with the > 1,000 ha days separately, and documented as a model-discrepancy term. Validate on the largest runs; curing and FMC perturbation sizes unvalidated.

- [ ] **Structure-spread display vs the Jasper result.** The owner reinstated per-building display on 2026-10-10 (D3 reversed; [R6] addendum). The Jasper check found building-level precision of 63 % (55-84 %) and no gain over a distance band [R10], so keep the "illustrative — not validated" legend caveat and state the 30 m cutoff dependence (count 124-593 over cutoffs of 20-45 m on Jasper).
- [ ] **Structure front contact is made at grid resolution** (after `11c1230`, [R8]): contact no longer depends on a footprint's position in its cell, but on the 50 m grid a building is reached when the front reaches a cell next to its cells (median 22-42 m, up to ~70 m, from the footprint), and `wildland_contact_m` has no effect below one cell. Finer options for later, each with its own re-run: coverage-fraction masking, or a finer fuel grid near buildings.
- [ ] **Structure spread in the app (from 2026-10-10):** opt-in option, counts, chart and per-building map layer (D3 superseded by the owner, decisions log 2026-10-10); still to check with users that the per-building map is read as modelled involvement and not as a loss forecast; not yet in any export or ICS 209 (by design). Building mask on the fuel grid still covers only the 4 nearest neighbourhoods, while structure units may come from the whole run area (the building-cell contact rule needs no special case for it: outside the mask a building's own cells can burn; a mask over every building would change the wildland run itself, so it is not done here). 12,025 of the 346,238 footprints have their centroid outside the Edmonton fuel grid's box and never become units.
- [ ] **Ember ignition (2026-10-10, [R16]): owner decision needed on the design fire for the ember stage.** At the 150 kW/m² default (D1) a building's whole fire emits about as many embers per m² of footprint as the threshold needs at 40 mph, so ember ignition almost never happens; GR' = 10 [T] was set by its authors with the 400 kW/m² curve (Qin 2025 p.147, Table 6.1). Options: keep 150 (ember stage effectively inert, honest to D1), use 400 for the ember stage only, or wait for a Canadian test. Also open: the wind heights in the Himoto and Sardoy PDFs are inferred from check values (C17, C18); Sardoy applied to 50 m FBP cells at crown-fire intensities is outside its shown range; the 250-500 m Jasper losses are not explained by this short-range model.
- [ ] **Burn-out duration (2026-10-10, [R19]):** 66 / 70 min is the published design fire's length, not a measured burning time for Edmonton construction (Qin25 p.146: ~50 min for a model house; FSJ104686 p.2: tens of minutes to hours). It binds only for crossings longer than that, so results barely depend on it; if a later stage (WU-E heat flux, embers at 400 kW/m²) is driven by the HRR curve, its shape matters more than its end. Owner decision open on whether burn-out stays on by default (on now, for one design fire across stages).
- [ ] **API memory baseline:** without structure spread a grid run with buildings already peaks at ~1.09 GB locally, mostly the `BuildingIndex` holding all 346,238 footprints as raw GeoJSON dicts (plus the JSON load); compact coordinate arrays would cut it. The structure-spread guard (60,000 units) is sized from local measurements, not on the Fly machine; the live API was not exercised with `structure_spread` after the fix (no deploy in this PR) [R11].
- [ ] **Ensemble moisture perturbation rework (M11, [R15]; owner: a separate later step, not done here).** The UI applies the FFMC perturbation (sd 7.2) to the *starting* FFMC, and the 17:00 spin-up erases it: starting values 85 ± 7.2 run through the spin-up on a typical July diurnal cycle end within **±0.3 FFMC by 14:00-16:00** ([R15] §3). Forecast T/RH, the real source of FFMC error during a run, are not perturbed; the 1.5 inflation was fitted with this set-up, so it absorbs the gap, but members do not experience "FFMC sd 7.2". The measured GEM day-1 biases (FFMC −3.1, log wind speed −0.15; `docs/validation.md`) are not applied. Fix: perturb the hourly T/RH stream with sizes from `input_errors.py` (same GEM-vs-station data) or perturb FFMC at the start of the burning period; apply or document the biases; refit the inflation.
- [ ] **DMC rain-phase form:** Van Wagner (1987) eq 16 as printed vs cffdrs's 20 + 280/exp(0.023 P) (≤ 0.064 DMC on rain days). Settle with the FTR-33 PDF (not on disk), which would also confirm the −2.8 °C DC floor in the 1985 FORTRAN.
- [x] **Alberta permanent UTC-6 from 2026-11-01 (owner confirmed 2026-10-10):** done 2026-10-18 on `fix/alberta-permanent-utc6` with the Pyra companion PR ([R57], §3). Correction to this item: Alberta's noon LST does **not** move to 18 UTC; CWFIS keeps 19 UTC (tz_correct -7). Still open: re-check the observation hour once November reports arrive and at season start (~1 March 2027) by matching CWFIS daily values to ECCC hourly records; if Edmonton matches 18 UTC, set `FIRESIM_AB_FWI_LST_OFFSET_H=6` (Fly) and Pyra `AB_FWI_LST_OFFSET_FROM_2026_11_01 = 6`.
- [ ] **Pyra tier, known gaps (2026-10-10):** (a) after noon LST with CWFIS empty, Pyra uses an MSC SWOB noon observation before GEM; FireSim goes straight to GEM (message says so); (b) Alberta only: Pyra's BC page (BCWS noon mirror tier, PST) is not reproduced, BC points use the CWFIS chain; (c) Pyra's IDW mode (CWFIS + AEF blend) and a browser's older holding cache are not reproduced (default page only); (d) the station list and rules are a copy of Pyra `38aee4f`: re-export the list and re-run the parity test with `PYRA_DIR` after any Pyra change to the chain; (e) the frontend computes its live ISI/FWI from the wind rounded to 1 km/h, so the panel can differ from the API (and Pyra) in the last decimal of ISI/FWI on windy dry days — the starting FFMC/DMC/DC are exact; (f) done 2026-10-18 ([R57]): dated clock rules in both, noon LST 19 UTC kept; (g) the peak-burn ISI/FWI (16:00 MDT wind) are returned but not used by the run.
- [ ] **Current weather after the 2026-10-10 fixes:** outside Alberta (or when Pyra's chain has no value) the off-season value is still a one-day cold start from 85/6/15 (DMC, DC, BUI far too low except after snowmelt; labelled); a carry-over chain (stepping archive codes forward with GEM noon weather, as Pyra does) or overwinter DC (VW 1987 eq 27) would replace it. Noon LST uses a province table (AB UTC-7 = CWFIS tz_correct; dated and configurable from 2026-11-01, [R57]); Saskatchewan's north-west (-7) and the other mixed provinces now follow the nearest CWFIS station (2026-10-18, §3). Hourly precipitation is applied one hour late in the forecast stream (M12). DMC/DC still fixed within a run. The input-error climatology (`input_errors.py`, May-September noons) was not rerun for the DC fix; noon temperatures ≤ −2.8 °C are rare in that window.
- [ ] **NWP literature gap:** no GEM/HRDPS model paper or Canadian fire-weather forecast verification study is held ([R15] §5); FireSim's own GEM-vs-station error climatology is the only forecast-error evidence.
